# VeriGo — Parking Lot

Items identified during the staged review that are real, but deliberately not being acted on right now — either deferred by explicit choice, or waiting on a decision from you. Tracked here so nothing gets lost, and to be summarized in full at the end of the whole staged process.

Each entry: what it is, why it's parked, where the full detail lives. The two sections immediately below are the tidy, at-a-glance view; the dated sections further down are the full narrative record each item's row links back to.

**P37 and P47 — both resolved, 2026-09-11.** The `ControlStatus` enum-name collision that reproducibly broke new-org registration on a fresh database is fixed, and the identical bug pattern found 8 more times while fixing it (`AlertStatus`, `AssessmentStatus` — three-way, `CustomerType`, `NoteType`, `RecommendationPriority`, `RecommendationStatus`, `ReviewOutcome`, `TrainingStatus`) is fixed too, all in one batch — see "Resolved" below for both. **All of this is very likely to have been failing in production**, since every collision was deterministic (whichever table's column the baseline migration's `create_all()` processed first won the shared type, and that ordering doesn't vary between environments built from the same code) — worth a live check on `veri-go.com.au` (a registration, and a business-entity onboarding session) to confirm, and applying this session's two new migrations (`96e0b54fc153_split_controlstatus_enum_type`, `0d8a1fc0b65b_split_p47_duplicate_enum_types`) there once confirmed.

### C4 — Naming cleanups
**Status:** Resolved, 2026-09-16 — see "Stage 17 — fourth pass" below.
**What it was:** `app/models/governance.py` actually only contained the policy domain (misleading next to `governance_controls.py`/`governance_training.py`/`governance_customisation.py`); `identity_verification.py` vs `identity_verification_service.py` were two different live scoring systems distinguished only by a `_service` suffix that carried no real meaning.
**What was done:** `git mv`'d `governance.py` → `governance_policies.py` (matching its siblings' naming convention), `identity_verification.py` → `document_verification.py` (single-document field verification: name/DOB/expiry match against one uploaded document), and `identity_verification_service.py` → `identity_composite_score.py` (the six-category OCR/manual/PEP/sanctions/adverse-media/company composite score, matching its own docstring's title). Every import site across `app/`, `tests/`, and `main.py`'s side-effect model-registration list updated; no behaviour change.
**Detail:** `app/models/governance_policies.py`, `app/services/document_verification.py`, `app/services/identity_composite_score.py`, and every file importing from them.

## Parked from the mypy CI-gate fix (2026-09-08)

Making CI's mypy check actually block the build (fix-now item, see resolved section below) meant triaging all 184 pre-existing errors it had been silently ignoring. Most were either genuine bugs (fixed, see below) or annotation gaps (fixed). A handful of things surfaced along the way that need a decision or more scope than a type-checker fix, rather than a guess:

### P4 — "Under review" policy status was mapped to three real sub-stages (judgment call, worth confirming)
**Status:** Already resolved when found (the fix described below was made at the same time this was parked) — kept as a flagged judgment call, not a pending action. Re-checked 2026-09-16 while working the "ready to pick up now" parking-lot batch: still just a documented judgment call with no bug attached, so no code change was needed or made.
**What:** `board_reporting_service.py`'s `_policies_section()` used `PolicyLifecycleStatus.under_review`/`.approved`, neither of which exist (real lifecycle: draft → internal_review → compliance_review → pending_approval → published → periodic_review → superseded → archived). Fixed `.approved` → `.published` (clear match, per the enum's own comment). For `.under_review`, mapped it to the three real intermediate stages (`internal_review`, `compliance_review`, `pending_approval`) grouped together, since the board report's "under review" bucket is presented as a single count.
**Why parked:** That grouping is a reasonable reading of intent, not a certainty — if board reports should distinguish "with the author" from "with the MLRO" from "awaiting Board sign-off," this needs revisiting. Revisit only if you want that finer split; no bug to fix in the meantime.
**Detail:** `app/services/board_reporting_service.py`, `_policies_section()`.

### P5 — `app/models/*.py` relationships aren't `Mapped[]`-typed
**Status:** `Column()` side resolved 2026-09-16 — see "P5/C2 pass, 2026-09-16" below. The 184 `relationship()` declarations are the one remaining piece, deliberately left for a follow-up pass (see that section for why).
**What:** All of `app/models/` uses SQLAlchemy's classic `Column()`/`relationship()` declarative style. Without an explicit `Mapped[X]` (or `Mapped[list[X]]`) annotation on the left-hand side, the SQLAlchemy mypy plugin can't infer a relationship's scalar-vs-collection Python type, or a `Float`/`Numeric`/`Enum` column's Python type on either read or write — every access type-checks as `Mapped[Any]` or an unresolved `SQLCoreOperations[_N] | _N`, masking real bugs a properly-typed access would catch. `mypy.ini` disables the `[misc]` error code project-wide for `app.models.*` to suppress the (harmless, SQLAlchemy always resolves the real type correctly at runtime) declaration-line noise this produces once a column *is* annotated. That suppression stays in place for now — re-enabling `[misc]` for `app.models.*` and triaging whatever it newly surfaces (real bug vs. annotation gap, the same exercise as the original CI-gate fix) is a distinct, much larger decision not bundled into this mechanical pass.
**Detail:** `mypy.ini`'s `[mypy-app.models.*]` section; the codemod and its output are described in "P5/C2 pass, 2026-09-16" below.

## Parked from Stage 7 (AML/CTF Program Module, 2026-09-08)

Fixing industry selection (P6, resolved below) required understanding this codebase's full "org identity / AML program" architecture, which turned out to have more overlapping pieces than P7 alone described. The pieces below are real but out of this stage's bounded scope.

*(P11, the "seeded controls are invisible to the real controls UI" finding originally parked here — P7 made concrete — is resolved — see Resolved below.)*

*(P12, the two-independent-AML-Program-systems finding originally parked here, is resolved — see Resolved below.)*

### P13 — `OnboardingWizard`'s "first customer" step maps industries to the wrong values — confirmed inert, not just wrong
**Status:** Parked — re-investigated during the critical/non-critical triage below; downgraded from "correctness bug" to "dead code," since fixing it would have zero live effect.
**What:** `customerIndustryFor()` (`web/components/OnboardingWizard.tsx`) matches against slugs like `'digital-currency-exchange'`, `'remittance-provider'` — none of which exist in `web/lib/industries.ts`'s real `id` values, so every real selection falls through to `'other'`. Originally flagged as a customer-misclassification risk. On closer inspection: the `industry` key `createFirstCustomer()` sends is not a field `CustomerCreate` (`app/schemas/customer.py`) defines at all — Pydantic silently drops it as an unrecognised extra field, and the real `industry_sector` column isn't settable at creation either. So the wizard's industry choice has never reached any real customer field or fed the risk engine (which doesn't read `industry_sector`), independent of the slug bug. Fixing the slug mapping would change what gets silently discarded, not what gets stored.
**Why parked:** No live effect to fix — genuinely cosmetic/dead code, confirmed rather than assumed. Revisit only if `CustomerCreate` ever gains a real `industry` field this should feed.
**Detail:** `web/components/OnboardingWizard.tsx`'s `customerIndustryFor()`; `app/schemas/customer.py`'s `CustomerCreate` (no `industry` field); `app/models/customer.py`'s `industry_sector` column (update-only, not settable at creation).

### P14 — Onboarding wizard's industry dropdown offers a non-selectable option
**Status:** Parked — trivial, cosmetic.
**What:** `web/lib/industries.ts` includes `'reporting_group'` as an `IndustryId` ("structural, not an AUSTRAC industry type" per its own comment), and it appears in the wizard's/start-trial's industry `<select>`. Selecting it now correctly 422s against `POST /organisations/{id}/select-industry` (real `IndustryType` enum validation, added this stage), rather than silently succeeding — an improvement over before, but the option shouldn't be user-selectable there in the first place.
**Why parked:** One-line frontend filter; not encountered as a live bug in this stage's own testing, just noticed while reading the industry list.
**Detail:** `web/lib/industries.ts`'s `industries` array; both consumers (`OnboardingWizard.tsx`, `StartTrialForm.tsx`).

### Resolved while reading — "Money Exchange" sector mapping
**What:** Google Drive has a dedicated `VERIGO_MONEYEXCHANGE_*` document suite, but there is no distinct `IndustryType` enum value for it — it isn't obviously `remittance` (a different regulated activity on paper: sending money overseas vs. exchanging currency) and doesn't fit any other existing category.
**Resolved:** per your direction — a money changer belongs under `IndustryType.remittance`; it's the same designated service, just the cash-handling variant. No new enum value needed. `app/templates/aml/industries/remittance.py` already carries the cash/TTR-obligated content (`has_ttr_obligation=True`, `ttr_procedures`) alongside the electronic-transfer content — its docstring now says explicitly that it covers both variants, so this isn't rediscovered as ambiguous later.
**Detail:** `app/templates/aml/industries/remittance.py`.

### P27 — No production transaction-ingestion path beyond manual entry
**Status:** Parked — this is later-stage territory (batch/API ingestion, core-banking integration), not a Stage 8 gap.
**What:** Every transaction that reaches the monitoring pipeline today arrives via a human filling in the manual entry form (`web/app/monitoring/page.tsx`) and `POST /transactions/`. **Why parked:** Explicitly noted rather than silently treated as in-scope — building real ingestion is a substantial, separate piece of work (format handling, idempotency, partial-failure semantics, likely per-integration) that belongs to a later stage of the original plan, not a fix folded into this one.

## Resolved (moved out of the active parking lot, kept here for the full-process history)

### P1 — Independent review "due" notifications have no date to key off
**Parked:** 2026-09-08, as "needs a product decision, not a mechanical fix." **Resolved:** 2026-09-08 (same session), while finishing the second mypy CI-gate pass (see "Second mypy CI-gate pass" below) — enabling the SQLAlchemy mypy plugin re-surfaced the exact same underlying bug from the type-checker's side, which forced the decision.
**What it was:** `check_independent_review_due()` (`app/services/notification_scheduler.py`) filtered on `IndependentReview.target_completion` — a column that never existed on the model, and there was no field anywhere (model, create/update API schema) for setting a review's target completion date in the first place. Its own try/except silently caught the `AttributeError` on every scheduled run, so this notification had never fired once.
**What was done:** Added `IndependentReview.target_completion_date` (migration `d2e3f4a5b6c7`) and wired it into `ReviewCreate`/`ReviewUpdate` and the review response dict, so it's an explicit, settable field rather than an inferred/derived one — the simpler of the two options raised when this was parked, chosen because deriving it from a fixed AUSTRAC cadence would need a firmer read on how that cadence should be tracked (per-org override? per-review-type?) than was available here.
**Detail:** `tests/test_independent_review_target_completion_smoke.py`; `app/models/independent_review.py`; `app/api/routes/independent_review.py`.

### P2 — Retention purge report only covered 1 of 5 KYC verification tables
**Parked:** 2026-09-08, as "a well-defined, bounded piece of work, but expanding it wasn't part of the [original] crash fix." **Resolved:** 2026-09-08 (same session), as the one clearly worthwhile, self-contained item left in the parking lot once the CI mypy fix was done.
**What it was:** `generate_purge_report()` (`app/services/retention_service.py`) only swept `CustomerIdentityDocument`, the most fundamental of the five real KYC verification tables (identity document, selfie, address, phone, email) — the other four were never included in the purge-eligibility sweep, so a compliance officer reviewing the report would see it as complete when it wasn't.
**What was done:** Generalised the sweep into a loop over all five KYC verification models (all share the same `org_id`/`created_at` shape), each reported under its own `scope` label (`kyc_identity_document`, `kyc_selfie_verification`, etc.) rather than collapsing them into one ambiguous `kyc_record` bucket.
**Detail:** `app/services/retention_service.py`, `generate_purge_report()`; `tests/test_retention_purge_report_crash_smoke.py`.

### P3 — Registration left every real user's org identity split across three NULLs
**Parked:** 2026-09-08, as "two parallel org-id concepts, needs a deliberate look before picking a side." **Resolved:** 2026-09-08 (same session), after scanning the parking lot ahead of Stage 5 and digging one level deeper into this specific item, since it sits directly in Stage 5's territory.
**What it turned out to be:** Not a judgment call between two legitimate conventions — a real, confirmed, live bug. A real registration through the actual API (`POST /auth/register`, then inspecting the resulting row directly) showed `org_id` set but `industry_id` and `primary_organisation_id` permanently `NULL`, because the frontend never sends `organisation_name` (confirmed: zero references anywhere under `web/`) and the code path that attaches a new user to their own org as "owner" — RBAC membership, `primary_organisation_id`, `industry_id` — was gated behind that field. This broke onboarding-session creation outright (`Customer.org_id` stamped from the `NULL` field, hitting its NOT NULL constraint), and silently broke documents/billing/storage/connectors/IFTI/analytics scoping and org-membership RBAC for every real user.
**What was done:** Extracted the attach-user-to-org logic (`org_service.py`'s `create_organisation()`) into a reusable `attach_owner()`, and made `register()` always call it on the one org it creates for a new user — self-serve signup still always creates a brand-new org and never joins an existing one (the deliberate anti-privilege-escalation design already documented there is unchanged), it now just consistently owns it across all three identity fields instead of only one.
**Detail:** `tests/test_register_identity_consistency_smoke.py`; `app/api/routes/auth.py`'s `register()`; `app/services/org_service.py`'s `attach_owner()`.

### Fix-now items — unauthenticated sanctions endpoint + mypy CI gate
**Parked:** never — these were the two "fix now" candidates agreed on 2026-09-08 in the same session as the full gap list.
**What was done:**
- Deleted `app/api/routes/sanctions.py` (`POST /sanctions/screen` had no auth dependency at all) and its registration in `main.py` — the authenticated `screening.py` module already covers this need.
- Made CI's mypy step actually block the build (was `--no-error-summary || true`, silently ignoring every error). That meant triaging all 184 pre-existing errors mypy had been suppressing, down to 0.
**What the triage surfaced along the way — nine confirmed, previously-undetected production bugs, each fixed with a regression test that reproduced the failure against the pre-fix code:**
- AUSTRAC SMR/IFTI/TTR filing-deadline reminder emails have never fired for any organisation (`ReportStatus.pending_review` doesn't exist; the check silently logged an error and returned 0 every time).
- Onboarding session creation was broken on all three of its entry points — manual creation and both CSV/Excel bulk import (a tuple-unpacking mismatch, then a `current_user.user_id` typo — `User` has no such attribute, it's `.id`).
- `GET /aml-program/risk-assessments` and `/compliance-status` were unreachable — shadowed by `GET /aml-program/{program_id}` registered earlier, so both 404'd via the wrong handler.
- Six list endpoints (screening, aml-program versions/risk-assessments, governance training assignments/records) 500'd on every call — `Pagination` has no `.limit` attribute.
- A sixth confirmed instance of the recurring "per-org admin role mistaken for the global super-admin flag" bug class, this time in `analytics.py` — any org's own admin could read another organisation's dashboard/customer/transaction analytics via the `?industry_id=` override.
- Compliance notification emails (deadline alerts, training overdue, policy review due, etc.) and compliance-calendar reminder emails have never actually sent — both silently swallowed by a broad `except Exception`, calling a real email function with a signature that didn't match, or importing a function that doesn't exist at all.
- Document versioning (`POST /documents/{id}/new-version`) has never worked — the service function didn't accept the version-tracking parameters the route passed it, and the call was also missing `await`.
- A recurring pattern of code referencing enum members that were never actually defined (e.g. `AlertStatus.open`/`.cleared`/`.false_positive` on the wrong `AlertStatus` — there are two enums of that name in this codebase — `CDDLevel.edd` instead of `.enhanced`, `CaseStatus.closed`/`.withdrawn` where there are five separate `closed_*` states and no "withdrawn" at all) broke board reporting, benchmarking, and the reporting-group dashboard, mostly silently (wrapped in broad exception handlers).
**Detail:** commits on `claude/verigo-repo-inspection-kffwrd` from the sanctions-endpoint deletion through the final mypy-clean commit; each has its own regression test file under `tests/` named for the bug it covers.

### Second mypy CI-gate break — CI's real command still had 1545 errors
**Parked:** never — the user reported the "Backend (Python 3.11)" CI job failing and asked for it to be diagnosed and fixed.
**What it was:** The "Fix-now" mypy-clean pass above had verified `mypy` through an isolated `uv tool install` environment with no access to the project's real dependencies — combined with `--ignore-missing-imports`, that silently treated SQLAlchemy (and everything else) as untyped `Any`, so the "0 errors" it reported never matched what CI's `pip install mypy && mypy app/ --ignore-missing-imports --no-strict-optional` actually saw. CI's real command reported 1545 errors, entirely because no `mypy.ini` existed to enable `sqlalchemy.ext.mypy.plugin` — without it, every classic `Column()`-style model attribute type-checks as `Column[T]` instead of `T`, producing thousands of false positives that drown out real bugs.
**What was done:** Added `mypy.ini` enabling the plugin (with `[misc]` disabled for `app.models.*` — see P5 above). That alone cut 1545 errors to 634; from there, the same real-bug-vs-annotation-gap triage from the first pass, across four more commits, brought it to 0 — matching CI's exact command for the first time.
**What the triage surfaced along the way — real, confirmed, previously-undetected bugs, each fixed with a regression test proven to fail pre-fix via `git stash`:**
- `AuditLog(entity_type=..., entity_id=..., detail=...)` — none of those kwargs exist (real fields: `event_type`/`object_type`/`object_id`/`new_value`) — crashed every governance control create/update, test finalisation, and policy update/workflow transition.
- `ControlRemediationAction` had no `control_id` column at all, though every remediation route built or filtered by one — the entire governance remediation subsystem crashed on every call. Added the column via migration.
- Three independent instances of `SomeModel(metadata=...)` — `metadata` isn't a real column on any model (it's the SQLAlchemy declarative class's own `MetaData` object) but doesn't raise either, so each one silently discarded the value instead of persisting it: `record_security_event()` (meant every login/MFA/password-change security event, ~20 call sites in `auth.py`, was silently dropped), `customer_workflow.py`'s `_transition()` (every customer workflow transition's audit event lost its metadata), and `security_monitor.py`'s role-change audit (always read back `None` instead of the real target/from/to role).
- `security_monitor.py`'s brute-force detection used `SecurityEvent.ip_address is not None` inside `.filter(...)` — a Python identity check against the column descriptor (always `True`), not a SQL predicate, so NULL-IP failed logins were never actually excluded from the count.
- `examination_pack_service.py` (the AUSTRAC examination pack generator): `AMLProgram.name`, `RiskAssessment.assessed_at`, `Policy.lifecycle_status`, and a second independent `IFTIERecord.org_id` instance (real fields: none/`version`, `assessment_date`, `status`, `industry_id`) — each guarded by `getattr`/`hasattr` so none crashed, they just silently rendered null/empty in every examination pack ever generated.
- `board_reporting_service.py`: `SMRReport.smr_lodged`, `TrainingAssignment.status`, `ControlTest.tested_at`, and a third independent `IFTIERecord.org_id` instance — every board/quarterly/annual compliance snapshot crashed building the SMR, training, controls, or regulatory-reporting sections.
- `IndependentReview.target_completion` — see P1 above (moved from parked to resolved in this same pass).
**Detail:** commits on `claude/verigo-repo-inspection-kffwrd` from the `mypy.ini` addition through the final 0-error commit; each real bug has its own regression test file under `tests/`.

### P8 — No organisation ever created through a real code path had a working AML/CTF Solution
**Parked:** never — found and fixed in the same pass, while running a live end-to-end trace of both risk engines to verify Stage 6.
**What it was:** `seed_aml_solution()` (`app/templates/aml/factory.py`) and `seed_risk_framework()` (`app/templates/risk/factory.py`) — which create the `AMLSolution` row, the org's `AMLProgram` document, and its `RiskFramework` (with industry-seeded `RiskCategory`/`RiskFactor` rows) — have existed since early in this staged process, but were never actually called from any real code path (only referenced inside their own module docstrings). Every organisation ever created through real registration (`POST /auth/register`) or admin-facing org creation (`POST /organisations`) got a bare `Organisation` row and nothing else: `GET /aml-program`, `GET /risk/framework`, `GET /governance/controls`, and every other endpoint gated on having an `AMLSolution` 404'd with "Complete onboarding and industry selection first," permanently, for every real user — with no "complete onboarding" endpoint anywhere that could have triggered the seeding either. This is why so many of this session's earlier regression tests had to manually construct an `AMLSolution` row in their own setup rather than going through the real flow: the real flow never produced one.
**What was done:** Wired both seed functions into `org_service.py`'s `attach_owner()` — the one function already called from both real org-creation paths — via a new `_seed_aml_solution_and_risk_framework()` helper, guarded on `AMLSolution.org_id`'s unique constraint so it's a safe no-op if `attach_owner()` is ever called twice for the same org.
**Detail:** `app/services/org_service.py`'s `attach_owner()`/`_seed_aml_solution_and_risk_framework()`; `tests/test_org_creation_seeds_aml_solution_smoke.py`.

### P9 — Customer-level risk engine carried no governance disclaimer
**Parked:** never — found and fixed in the same pass, while confirming both risk engines against Stage 6's requirements.
**What it was:** The org-level EWRA engine (`app/api/routes/risk_assessment.py`) has always returned a `DISCLAIMER` — "this framework is a configurable tool only... the platform does not determine final risk ratings... or accept liability for risk outcomes" — on every response, and requires it be acknowledged at approval. The per-customer onboarding risk engine (`app/services/customer_risk_engine.py`, `app/api/routes/customer_workflow.py`'s `run_risk_assessment()`/`get_risk_profile()`) had no equivalent anywhere — a real gap given this is the engine that actually gates individual customers into CDD vs EDD.
**What was done:** Added a matching `CUSTOMER_RISK_DISCLAIMER` constant (`app/schemas/customer_workflow.py`) and a `disclaimer` field (with that text as its default) on both `RiskAssessmentResponse` and `RiskProfileResponse`, so every `/assess-risk` and `/risk-profile` response now carries it, same as the EWRA engine.
**Detail:** `app/schemas/customer_workflow.py`; `tests/test_stage6_risk_assessment_engine_live_trace.py`.

### P6 — `Organisation.industry_type` was hardcoded to `other` on every real org-creation path
**Parked:** 2026-09-08 (Stage 6), as "needs a product decision (what selects industry, and when)." **Resolved:** 2026-09-08 (Stage 7) — Stage 7's own first requirement is "select industry," so the decision this was waiting on was made by the stage itself.
**What was done:** Added `POST /organisations/{org_id}/select-industry`, which sets `Organisation.industry_type` and re-seeds the AML Solution (Program + Risk Framework) from the correct industry template — safely: only allowed while nothing's been customised yet (program still draft v1.0, no risk assessment started), otherwise 409, so it can't silently destroy real work. Wired the existing (but wrongly-wired) onboarding wizard's industry step to call it.
**A bigger gap found in the process:** there was no reachable frontend page anywhere for a new visitor to actually register — `POST /auth/register` had zero UI callers (only a login page existed). The onboarding wizard this fix needed to wire up was unreachable regardless. Fixed by wiring the existing, fully-designed-but-non-functional `start-trial` signup form (had no `onSubmit` handler at all) to actually call registration.
**Detail:** `app/services/org_service.py`'s `select_industry()`; `app/api/routes/organisations.py`; `web/app/start-trial/StartTrialForm.tsx`; `web/components/OnboardingWizard.tsx`; `tests/test_stage7_select_industry_smoke.py`. See P11-P14 above for what this surfaced but didn't fix.

### C3 — Risk-threshold consolidation
**Parked:** 2026-09-08. **Resolved:** 2026-09-08 (same session), per your explicit direction to standardise on ISO 31000 style.
**What it was:** Two genuinely live, overlapping "customer risk score" engines (`customer_risk_engine.py` vs `risk_matrix_service.py`), plus a third undocumented ad hoc scale in a `/rescore` endpoint, each with different threshold boundaries — the same customer could get a different risk level depending which code path last touched them. Separately, the AUD $10,000 statutory TTR/CTR threshold was duplicated across ~20 sites.
**What was done:** `risk_engine.py`'s ISO 31000 methodology is now the single source of truth for both — a shared `risk_rating_pct()` function every scoring module rates against, and one `TTR_CTR_THRESHOLD_AUD` constant every threshold check references. Professional assessment scoring combined into the same rating function (with a documented, bump-able ceiling for future indicators). A real pre-existing bug surfaced and fixed along the way: a sanctions match forced the EDD workflow but not the numeric score to critical — now it does, verified live end-to-end.
**Real behavioural changes to be aware of:** customer risk classification boundaries are now stricter in some cases (a score that was "medium" can now be "high"); professional assessment classification is now more lenient in some cases (needs a relatively higher point total to reach "critical"). Existing stored risk levels aren't retroactively recomputed — this affects new scoring and rescoring going forward.
**Detail:** `STRUCTURE_REVIEW.md`, section C3.

### Transaction creation never actually ran the monitoring pipeline it claimed to
**Parked:** never — found and fixed in the same pass, tracing Stage 8's `Transaction → Rules → Alert → Case` pipeline live end to end.
**What it was:** `POST /transactions/` (`app/api/routes/transactions.py`'s `create_transaction()`) never called `run_monitoring()`. Its own docstring, and the sibling `/run-monitoring` endpoint's docstring, both claimed scoring happened "automatically... in production" — it didn't; every transaction sat completely unscored, with zero alerts ever generated, unless something separately called the manual re-evaluation endpoint afterward. Same "seeding function exists but is never wired in" shape as P8 (Stage 6) and the starter-rules gap below.
**What was done:** Added the `run_monitoring(txn, customer, db)` call to `create_transaction()`, alongside the (already-wired) automation-rules call. Corrected both docstrings to state what actually happens.
**Detail:** `app/api/routes/transactions.py`'s `create_transaction()`/`run_monitoring_on_transaction()`; `tests/test_stage8_transaction_creation_triggers_monitoring_smoke.py`.

### No organisation ever got any starter `MonitoringRule` rows — the "configurable rules" layer had nothing to configure
**Parked:** never — found and fixed in the same pass.
**What it was:** `MonitoringRule` (the real, no-code, per-org configurable rule engine the pipeline reads — distinct from the unrelated `AutomationRule` system, see P25 above) has a full CRUD API, but nothing ever seeded a starting set of rules for a new org. Every org started with zero rules; score-based behaviour-signal alerting still worked independently, but the configurable-rules layer Stage 8 explicitly asks for had no starting content — no seed to edit, just an empty list, for every real org.
**What was done:** Seeded 6 starter rules on org creation (`org_service.py`'s `attach_owner()`, via `_seed_default_monitoring_rules()`), one per indicator type the stage brief names: unusual amount, rapid movement, structuring, frequency anomaly, high-risk jurisdiction, high-risk customer. Marked `is_system_rule` (editable/disable-able, not deletable), matching the seeding discipline already used for controls/policies/approval questions.
**A second bug surfaced verifying it against a real payload:** `Transaction` has two overlapping country-pair column sets (`source_country`/`destination_country` vs `country_origin`/`country_destination`). Behaviour-signal scoring already checked all four; the rule-condition context (`monitoring_engine.py`'s `_build_txn_context()`) only ever exposed the first pair — so a transaction entered via the real frontend form (which sends `country_destination`, confirmed in `web/app/monitoring/page.tsx`) was invisible to every "high-risk jurisdiction" rule, seeded or user-created, regardless of this fix. Fixed alongside by falling back to the other pair when the first is unset.
**Detail:** `app/services/org_service.py`'s `_seed_default_monitoring_rules()`; `app/services/monitoring_engine.py`'s `_build_txn_context()`; `tests/test_stage8_default_monitoring_rules_seeded_smoke.py`.

### MLRO dashboard's "Close Case" button called the wrong endpoint and silently faked success on failure
**Parked:** never — found and fixed in the same pass, tracing the pipeline's investigation/outcome stage (`web/app/mlro/page.tsx`).
**What it was:** Three compounding issues in the case-management frontend. (1) The "Close Case" button called `POST /cases/{id}/status` (`transition_status`, compliance+) instead of `POST /cases/{id}/close` (`close_case`, mlro+) — the only endpoint that records `outcome`/`outcome_notes`/`closure_reason`/`closed_by`/`closed_at`; every case "closed" through the dashboard silently lost all of that data. (2) `updateStatus()` wrapped its fetch in `try { ... } catch {}`, discarding the response, then unconditionally updated local state and showed a success toast regardless of whether the backend call actually succeeded. (3) The page initialised and fell back to a hardcoded 5-case `DEMO_CASES` array whenever a real fetch returned zero cases — masking a genuinely empty, real organisation behind fabricated data indefinitely.
**What was done:** Added a proper close-case form (closing status, outcome, required closure reason, optional outcome notes) that calls the real `/close` endpoint; `updateStatus()` and the new close flow now check `response.ok` and only update state/show success on a genuine success, with an error toast otherwise; removed `DEMO_CASES` entirely — `fetchCases()` now sets real data unconditionally (including a real empty array) and surfaces a failed fetch as an error rather than silently keeping stale/fake data. `CreateCaseForm`'s matching fake-success-on-failure fallback (fabricated a synthetic case object) fixed the same way. Also fixed three smaller spots hardcoded to only recognise `closed_no_action` as "closed" (stats, the critical-cases list, the open-cases-by-age table) now that the UI can produce any of the 5 real closed statuses.
**Detail:** `web/app/mlro/page.tsx`; `tests/test_stage8_case_close_records_outcome_smoke.py` (backend contract — this frontend fix depends on, and this test file is also the first backend test coverage `app/api/routes/cases.py` has ever had).

### Critical/non-critical triage pass (2026-09-09): DPMS's wrong instruction, the alert→case bridge, and a second instance of the MLRO dashboard's bug pattern

You asked for the accumulated parking-lot items to be triaged into "fix now" vs. "stays parked," using the standard: does it undermine real AML/CTF compliance capability. Went through all 23 open items against that bar. Two were real, bounded corrections and got fixed; several others were investigated deeper specifically to check whether a "quick fix" was actually safe, and in every case turned out to need a genuine design or product decision instead — recorded in each item's entry above (P7/P11, P12, P25) rather than forced through. P13 turned out to be inert on closer inspection (see its entry) — nothing to fix.

### P21 — DPMS fallback template actively told the org to refuse the cash its designated service exists to handle
**Parked:** 2026-09-08, as "content-authoring work, sign-off wanted first." **Resolved:** 2026-09-09 — this specific instruction (not the full sector rewrite) met the "actively undermines compliance capability" bar on its own: it told a DPMS reporting entity to decline cash and that TTR reporting doesn't apply, the opposite of DPMS-01, the sector's own primary monitoring rule.
**What was done:** Added `app/templates/aml/industries/dpms.py` — a real, correct template covering the TTR/cash procedure (DPMS-01's actual $10,000 threshold rule) plus the concrete DPMS-specific monitoring thresholds already documented in this file's research (structuring near $10k, ≥$100k Director sign-off, 90-day buy-back, unknown provenance, cryptocurrency payment, mule-purchaser indicators, wholesale volume anomalies, sanctioned-origin metals). Both `IndustryType.bullion_dealers` and `IndustryType.precious_metals` now map to it instead of the generic `other.py` fallback. This is the correction, not the full sector rewrite — gemstone/watch-specific nuance, the remaining TMP rules, and a dedicated risk-library factor set (the rest of what P21's original research documented) stay in the content-rewrite queue at the same depth as the other thin sectors.
**Detail:** `app/templates/aml/industries/dpms.py`; `app/templates/aml/factory.py`'s industry mapping; `tests/test_dpms_ttr_template_fix_smoke.py` (confirmed failing against the old "other" mapping via `git stash` before the fix, per this session's standing regression-test discipline).

### P26 — No "create case from alert" action in the monitoring UI, despite full backend support
**Parked:** 2026-09-08, as "bounded frontend work, not attempted this pass." **Resolved:** 2026-09-09 — small and well-scoped as originally assessed, and worse than "missing" on closer inspection: the alert detail panel already had a "Create Case" link, but it pointed at `/mlro` with query params (`alert=...`) the MLRO page never reads, so it looked functional while silently dropping the alert linkage — an analyst clicking it got a blank new-case form with no idea the alert reference was lost.
**What was done:** Replaced the dead-end link with a real call to `POST /alerts/{alert_id}/create-case`, gated (like the Resolve/Escalate/Dismiss actions next to it) on the alert not already being closed. While in the same file, fixed the identical "fake success on failure" and "demo data masks empty state" bugs already found and fixed in the MLRO dashboard this stage: `doAction()` (resolve/dismiss/escalate) now checks `response.ok` and only reflects genuine success; `fetchData()` no longer falls back to a hardcoded 8-alert demo array, surfacing a real empty queue or a real fetch failure instead.
**Detail:** `web/app/monitoring/page.tsx`; `app/api/routes/alerts.py`'s `create_case_from_alert()`; `tests/test_alert_create_case_smoke.py` (first backend test coverage this endpoint has ever had, driven through the real transaction → monitoring → alert pipeline, not a hand-built alert row).

### P28 — Three independent backend implementations of IFTI reporting; two are entirely unreachable
**Parked:** 2026-09-09 (Stage 10), as a genuine architecture decision rather than a bug fix. **Resolved:** 2026-09-09, once you clarified the real intent: filling AUSTRAC's official IFTI-DRA Excel template by hand and lodging it via AUSTRAC Online — not an automated API/XML submission.
**Correcting this parking lot's own earlier framing:** the Stage 10 entry (and `docs/regulatory-reporting.md`) characterised `app/api/routes/ifti_e.py`/`IFTIERecord` as "the more AUSTRAC-schema-faithful system" and `app/api/routes/ifti.py`/`IFTIRecord` as a generic, non-schema-specific implementation. That was wrong. `ifti_e.py` covers **IFTI-E** — electronic/SWIFT funds transfers, submitted via AUSTRAC Connect's API, relevant to banks/ADIs. `ifti.py`/`IFTIRecord` covers a *different* AUSTRAC report subtype, **IFTI-DRA** (Designated Remittance Arrangement) — the one relevant to remittance dealers, this app's actual target client base — and is modelled on the real `IFTI-DRA-1-2.xsd` schema. Its `generate_ifti_excel()` produces a 112/115-column workbook whose "Instructions" sheet text is lifted near-verbatim from AUSTRAC's own IFTI-DRA spreadsheet, and whose column order and section headers match that template exactly — genuinely the schema-accurate system for this use case, not the less-faithful one. `app/api/routes/reports.py`'s third IFTI system had the maker-checker/audit workflow but, confirmed by grep, no Excel/CSV export capability at all — it could never produce a file usable for a real AUSTRAC lodgement.
**What was done:** Made `ifti.py`/`IFTIRecord` canonical. Expanded `IFTIStatus` (draft/ready/submitted → draft/under_review/approved/submitted/acknowledged/rejected) and added the missing workflow/audit columns (`reviewed_by`, `approved_by`, `approved_at`, `rejected_reason`, `submission_reference`, `acknowledged_at`, `due_date`, `reporter_austrac_id`) via a new Alembic migration. Ported the full maker-checker workflow onto `ifti.py` (review → approve → submit → acknowledge, reject → redraft, each audited under `entity_type=ifti_record`; reviewer ≠ approver enforced on approval; mandatory-field validation gates submission), and added a `POST /ifti/generate-from-transaction/{txn_id}` endpoint (via a new `generate_ifti_from_transaction()` in `ifti_service.py`) that `ifti.py` never had. Removed `reports.py`'s IFTI-only routes and helpers (its TTR/SMR/Filing-Register/ECDD sections are untouched); `reporting_summary()` now counts `IFTIRecord` instead of the retired `IFTIReport`. Repointed `web/app/reporting/page.tsx`'s IFTI calls from `/api/v1/reports/ifti*` to `/api/v1/ifti/*`. `ifti_e.py`/IFTI-E was explicitly left out of scope, per your answer. `IFTIReport`/`ifti_reports` (the model, not the routes) was deliberately left in place rather than dropped — `app/models/ifti_receipt.py`'s `IFTIReceipt` (a separate, unrelated customer-facing-receipt feature) still carries an optional FK to it.
**Detail:** `app/models/ifti.py`; `app/api/routes/ifti.py`; `app/services/ifti_service.py`; `app/services/reporting_service.py`; `app/api/routes/reports.py`; `web/app/reporting/page.tsx`; `alembic/versions/f1a2b3c4d5e6_ifti_dra_maker_checker_workflow.py`; `tests/test_ifti_dra_p28_workflow_smoke.py`, `tests/test_ifti.py`, `tests/test_ifti_status_tenant_scoping_smoke.py`; `docs/regulatory-reporting.md` (addendum). Two side-findings made while doing this, deliberately not fixed here to keep this pass scoped, were parked as P30/P31 — both now resolved (see Resolved below), and investigating them live surfaced P35, a much larger auth-dependency bug — also now resolved.

### P29 — ~35 route files had zero audit-trail coverage; the 7 AML/CTF-relevant ones it named are now fixed
**Parked:** 2026-09-09 (Stage 11), as a large, mechanical, not-boundable-in-one-pass gap, with `retention.py`, `screening.py`, `independent_review.py`, `board_reporting.py`, `monitoring.py`, `compliance_calendar.py`, and `onboarding.py` named as the AML/CTF-relevant subset. **Resolved:** 2026-09-09, on explicit instruction to fix it.
**Two corrections to the original framing, found doing the work:** (1) `retention.py` was flagged as "the standout next candidate given its actions are destructive/irreversible" — that overstated it. `generate_purge_report()` is explicitly dry-run-only (`# does NOT delete anything`) and no purge/delete-execution endpoint exists anywhere in the codebase; its real mutating surface is just retention-policy upserts and legal-hold create/release, both now audited. (2) `onboarding.py` already had its own audit trail — `OnboardingAuditLog`, reachable via `GET /onboarding/sessions/{id}/audit` — but that table is a separate silo never merged into the central `GET /audit/` endpoint (which only merges `legacy_audit_logs` and the newer `audit_logs` table), so onboarding events were invisible from the one place Stage 11 says a compliance officer should be able to see "what happened". Within its own silo, only 2 of ~7 lifecycle events were even logged (`invite_opened`, `session_cancelled` — not session creation, step submissions, final submission, reminders, or bulk import).
**What was done:** Added `audit_service.log_action()` calls to the real mutating endpoints across all 7 files (`app/api/routes/retention.py`, `screening.py`, `independent_review.py`, `board_reporting.py`, `monitoring.py`, `compliance_calendar.py`, `onboarding.py`) — 3, 9, 21, 9, 4, 6, and 9 endpoints respectively — following the same `_log()`-helper convention established in `cases.py`/`reports.py`/`kyc.py`/`transactions.py`. `onboarding.py`'s calls write to the central trail (`entity_type=onboarding_session`) alongside its existing `OnboardingAuditLog` silo, which is left as-is (still useful for its own purpose — a detailed step-by-step log for one session).
**Detail:** the 7 files above; `alembic` untouched (no schema change needed); `tests/test_p29_audit_coverage_sweep_smoke.py` covers one representative flow per file. Re-checking the ~28 files P29 didn't name found the "commercial/infrastructure, out of scope" bucketing wasn't complete either — reparked as P33 above.

### Independent Review and CO Quarterly Compliance Report aligned with their Google Drive AML/CTF templates
**Parked:** never — requested directly, found and fixed in the same pass (2026-09-09).
**What it was:** Two gaps against `VERIGO_GEN_Independent_Review_v1.docx` (VERIGO-GEN-IRF-01) and `VERIGO_GEN_CO_Quarterly_Report_v1.docx` (VERIGO-GEN-COR), both from the Google Drive AML/CTF document library. (1) `app/api/routes/independent_review.py` had no document export at all — unlike `board_reporting.py`, which has had `export-html`/`export-csv` since Stage 7 — so a review's findings could never be turned into the report document the template expects to hand to a Director. Its `FindingCategory` enum was also missing two of the template's mandatory review areas (Sanctions Screening, AUSTRAC Enrolment), which would otherwise have to be filed under "other". (2) `board_reporting.py` already has a `quarterly_compliance` `BoardReportType`, but `generate_quarterly_compliance_snapshot()` was just a pass-through to the same generic `_base_snapshot()` every other report type uses — none of the CO Quarterly Report template's specific sections (SMR/TTR/ECDD/Sanctions detail with late-lodgement and terrorism-24h tracking, open actions carried over from prior quarters, independent review status) were computed anywhere.
**What was done:** Added `FindingCategory.sanctions_screening`/`.austrac_enrolment` (+ migration). Added `GET /independent-reviews/{id}/export-html`, laid out to match the template: cover page (entity/ABN/reviewer/independence statement), an area-by-area executive-summary rating grid (worst open-finding severity per area, defaulting to Satisfactory when an area has none), detailed findings/recommendations, action plan, and a reviewer/Director sign-off block. Added `_smr_quarterly_detail()`, `_ttr_quarterly_section()`, `_ecdd_quarterly_section()`, `_sanctions_quarterly_section()`, and `_open_actions_quarterly_section()` (sourced from the Compliance Calendar rather than inventing a second tracking mechanism) to `board_reporting_service.py`, wired into `generate_quarterly_compliance_snapshot()`; `board_reporting.py`'s `export_html()` renders these as extra sections only when `report_type == "quarterly_compliance"` — the other three report types are unaffected (verified).
**What's still open:** No structured "compliance breach" model exists, so the CO Quarterly template's "Breaches Identified This Quarter" section and a few narrative-only Program/Regulatory-Updates items (new AUSTRAC guidance, legislative changes, AUSTRAC feedback) aren't computed from real data — left to the report's own free-text fields. `ECDDRecord` also can't distinguish "relationship exited" from "service declined". Parked as P34 above.
**Detail:** `app/models/independent_review.py`; `app/api/routes/independent_review.py`; `app/services/board_reporting_service.py`; `app/api/routes/board_reporting.py`; `alembic/versions/a1b2c3d4e5f7_independent_review_finding_categories.py`; `tests/test_ir_and_co_quarterly_template_alignment_smoke.py`.

### Stage 9 (Case Management): zero audit trail coverage across all 12 case-mutating endpoints
**Parked:** never — found and fixed while inspecting Stage 9 against its own explicitly-named requirement ("maintain a complete audit trail").
**What it was:** `app/api/routes/cases.py` never called `audit_service.log_action()`, unlike every other mutable AML/CTF record in this codebase (customers, alerts, reports, documents, organisations). A compliance officer could open, investigate, escalate, and close a case with no queryable record of who did what, when.
**What was done:** Added a `_log()` call to all 12 mutating endpoints (create, update, assign, escalate, status transition, close, notes, evidence, evidence verification, link-alert, SMR consider, SMR lodge). Note *content* deliberately excluded from audit entries — confidential/legal-privilege notes are access-restricted at the note level, and writing their content into the audit trail (readable by a broader set of roles) would have created a side channel around that restriction.
**A second gap found alongside:** `CaseAlert` links (which alert(s) drove a case) were write-only since the feature was first built. Added `GET /cases/{id}/alerts`, wired into the MLRO dashboard's case detail panel.
**Detail:** `app/api/routes/cases.py`; `tests/test_stage9_case_audit_trail_and_alerts_readback_smoke.py`; `docs/case-management.md`.

### Stage 10 (Australian Regulatory Reporting): only the final "submit" step was audited for IFTI/TTR/SMR reports; TTR and SMR had no way to ever reach "rejected"
**Parked:** never — found and fixed while inspecting Stage 10 against its own explicit requirement ("every report should maintain who created it, when, what changed, who approved it").
**What it was:** `app/api/routes/reports.py` called `audit_service.log_action()` only on the final `/submit` endpoint for each of IFTI, TTR, and SMR — 5 calls total across roughly 30 mutating endpoints. The entire maker-checker chain (draft, update, review, approve/MLRO sign-off, acknowledge, reject, redraft) was unaudited, the same shape as the Stage 9 finding above. Separately, `redraft_ttr` and `redraft_smr` both guard on `status == rejected`, but only IFTI had a working `/reject` endpoint — nothing could ever set a TTR or SMR report's status to `rejected`, making both reports' redraft endpoints permanently unreachable dead code.
**What was done:** Added a `_log()` call (same pattern as cases.py) to every remaining mutating endpoint across all three report types. Added `POST /reports/ttr/{id}/reject` and `POST /reports/smr/{id}/reject`, mirroring the pre-existing `reject_ifti`, making both reports' redraft paths reachable for the first time.
**A larger architecture finding surfaced investigating this:** three independent backend IFTI implementations exist, only one reachable from any frontend — parked as P28 above rather than fixed in this pass, since neither system alone satisfies Stage 10's requirements and reconciling them is a real design decision.
**Detail:** `app/api/routes/reports.py`; `tests/test_stage10_report_audit_trail_and_reject_smoke.py`; `docs/regulatory-reporting.md`.

### Stage 11 (Audit & Evidence): the audit page showed fabricated demo entries over a real, now much richer, audit trail; KYC decisions and transaction records were entirely unaudited
**Parked:** never — found and fixed while inspecting Stage 11 against its own charter ("what happened, when, who did it, why").
**What it was:** Three issues. (1) `web/app/audit/page.tsx` — the real, reachable audit trail UI — had the same demo-data-masking bug found repeatedly this session: a hardcoded 12-entry `DEMO_LOGS` array shown whenever a real fetch returned zero rows, and silently kept on any fetch error. For a page whose entire purpose is showing "what really happened" in a compliance/audit context, this is a more serious instance of the pattern than the other pages it was found on — someone reviewing "the audit trail" could be looking at entirely fabricated events. (2) The same page's entity-type and role filter lists were fictional: none of `"report"`/`"ecdd"`/`"kyc"`/`"transaction"` match any real `entity_type` string the backend actually writes (the real ones are `ifti_report`/`ttr_report`/`smr_report`/`case`/`customer`/`ecdd_record`/`document`/`aml_program`/`organisation`/`user`, split across two audit tables with inconsistent casing — snake_case vs PascalCase). (3) Checking real audit coverage found `app/api/routes/kyc.py`'s `review_kyc()` (the actual approve/reject decision on a customer's identity verification) and `app/api/routes/transactions.py`'s `create_transaction()`/`update_transaction()` (the core AML transaction record) had zero audit logging — exactly the "customer history"/"what happened to this transaction" Stage 11 names.
**What was done:** Removed `DEMO_LOGS`, so a real empty or failed fetch now shows a real empty/error state. Rebuilt `ENTITY_TYPES`/`ENTITY_COLOR`/`ENTITY_ICON`/`ACTOR_ROLES` from the real strings found via `grep -rho 'entity_type="..."' app/` plus the merged table's `object_type` values, and normalised casing once at fetch time so filtering/matching works regardless of which underlying table a given entry came from. Added `log_action()` calls to `review_kyc()` and to `create_transaction()`/`update_transaction()`.
**A larger finding surfaced doing this file-by-file:** roughly 35 other route files still have zero audit coverage — parked as P29 (not attempted in full this pass). P29 itself is now resolved (see its own entry below); the remaining files are reparked as P33.
**Detail:** `web/app/audit/page.tsx`; `app/api/routes/kyc.py`; `app/api/routes/transactions.py`; `tests/test_stage11_kyc_and_transaction_audit_smoke.py`; `docs/audit-evidence.md`.

### P7 / P11 — Governance seeding retargeted from the legacy `Control`/`AMLPolicy` models to `GovernanceControl`/`Policy`, the ones the real UI reads
**Parked:** 2026-09-08 (Stage 6, as P7; restated concretely as P11 in Stage 7), as a genuine migration with data-migration implications, not a bug fix. Re-investigated during the 2026-09-09 critical/non-critical triage, which confirmed the schema gap was real, and did not attempt it. **Resolved:** 2026-09-09, on explicit instruction ("start p7").
**What it was:** `seed_aml_solution()` (`app/templates/aml/factory.py`) created starter policies/controls in the legacy, UI-disconnected `app.models.aml_solution.AMLPolicy`/`Control` tables, while every real compliance officer manages controls and policies through `GET/POST /governance/controls`/`/governance/policies` — `app.models.governance_controls.GovernanceControl`/`app.models.governance.Policy`, a stricter, closed-enum model with no relationship to the legacy tables at all: `risk_area`/`policy_type` are closed enums (vs. free text), `control_method` is a mandatory field the legacy model has no equivalent for, and `control_owner`/`document_owner` are mandatory FKs to `User` (vs. an optional free-text `owner` on the legacy `Control`). Every freshly onboarded org's industry-specific starter controls/policies existed in the database but were invisible to the only UI/API that manages them.
**What was done:** Retargeted `seed_aml_solution()` to create `GovernanceControl`/`Policy` rows directly. Built explicit free-text→enum mapping tables for the two fields the legacy model left as free text: `policy_type` → `PolicyType` (18 values across `BASE_POLICIES` and all 8 industry templates' `extra_policies`) and `risk_area` → `ControlRiskArea` (22 values). Values with a precise equivalent were mapped to it (e.g. `sanctions`→`sanctions_screening`, `ifti`→`reporting_policy`); the 7 `risk_area` values and 4 `policy_type` values with no precise equivalent (`trust_accounts`, `corporate_structures`, `correspondent_banking`, `high_value_transactions`, `product_risk`, `third_party`, `agent_oversight`, `virtual_asset`) were deliberately routed to `ControlRiskArea.custom` (paired with `risk_area_custom` preserving the original label) or `PolicyType.other` — never force-mapped into an imprecise category, avoiding exactly the data-quality risk P11's re-investigation flagged. Added `PolicyType.independent_review_policy` (the one `BASE_POLICIES` entry, "Independent Review Policy", with no enum equivalent at all) via a new Alembic migration. `control_owner`/`document_owner`/`created_by` on every seeded row are set to the same `created_by`/`actor_id` already passed into `seed_aml_solution()` at both call sites (`org_service.py`'s `_seed_aml_solution_and_risk_framework()` and `select_industry()`) — always a real `User.id`. The now-mandatory `control_method` field (which the legacy model had no equivalent for) defaults to `ControlMethod.manual_review` for every seeded starter control — a placeholder until the org's compliance officer configures the real testing method, the same choice a human creating a control via `POST /governance/controls` would otherwise have to make manually. `control_ref`/`policy_number` numbering matches the real per-org, per-category counting the `/governance/controls`/`/governance/policies` create endpoints themselves use (`CONTROL_REF_PREFIX`/`POLICY_NUMBER_PREFIX`), tracked in-memory during the seed batch so industries whose templates map multiple free-text values onto the same enum (e.g. VASP's `transaction_monitoring` appearing in both `BASE_POLICIES` and its own `extra_policies`) get correctly incrementing refs rather than collisions. `select_industry()`'s wipe-and-reseed block now also deletes `GovernanceControl`/`Policy` rows (by `solution_id`) alongside the legacy tables, so an org changing industry doesn't accumulate stale governance rows from the old industry — and, as a side effect, cleans up any orphaned legacy `Control`/`AMLPolicy` rows left over from before this fix on its next industry change.
**Scope decision:** No backfill migration for orgs already seeded under the old code (their legacy rows are left in place, cleaned up only if/when that org next changes industry) — this is a fresh/test dataset, not a decision to silently migrate real production data; flag before assuming this is safe to do the same way for a live customer base.
**P12 explicitly not touched:** P12 (`AMLProgram` vs the wizard-driven, paywall-gated `AMLProgramRecord`) is a different pair of models — not part of this fix, remains parked above.
**Detail:** `app/templates/aml/factory.py`'s `seed_aml_solution()`; `app/models/governance.py`'s `PolicyType`/`POLICY_NUMBER_PREFIX`; `app/models/governance_controls.py`'s `ControlRiskArea`/`CONTROL_REF_PREFIX`; `app/services/org_service.py`'s `select_industry()`; `alembic/versions/b2c3d4e5f6a7_policy_type_independent_review.py`; `tests/test_p7_governance_seeding_smoke.py`.

### P30 / P31 — Reporting dashboard's demo-data masking and summary shape mismatch fixed; live browser testing surfaced a much bigger auth bug (P35)
**Parked:** 2026-09-09 (found doing P28, kept out of scope to keep that pass bounded). **Resolved:** 2026-09-09, on explicit instruction.
**What it was:** `web/app/reporting/page.tsx` had the same demo-data-masking bug already fixed on the MLRO, monitoring, and audit pages this session — `DEMO_REPORTS`/`DEMO_SUMMARY` shown whenever a real fetch returned zero rows or failed, kept forever regardless of whether the org genuinely had zero reports. Separately, `reporting_summary()` (`app/services/reporting_service.py`) returned a shape (`{ifti, ttr, smr, overdue: {ifti, ttr, smr}, total_filed}`) unrelated to what the frontend's `Summary` interface (`total`/`by_type`/`by_status`/`overdue`/`due_soon`/`draft`/`under_review`/`submitted`) reads, keyed by `str(StatusEnum.member)` (e.g. `"IFTIStatus.draft"`, not `"draft"`) — the frontend's own empty-check (`if (d.total !== undefined)`) could never pass.
**What was done:** Rewrote `reporting_summary()` to return the exact shape the frontend already expects, using real enum `.value` strings and merging IFTI/TTR/SMR counts (`IFTIStatus` and `ReportStatus` share the same value set: draft/under_review/approved/submitted/acknowledged/rejected). `overdue`/`due_soon` are now computed per-report across all three types (due within 3 days and not yet in a terminal status = due soon; past due and not terminal = overdue). Removed `DEMO_REPORTS`/`DEMO_SUMMARY` from `reporting/page.tsx`; a real empty or failed fetch now shows a real empty/error state (toast on total failure), matching the pattern already applied elsewhere this session. Updated `test_ifti_dra_p28_workflow_smoke.py`'s `test_reporting_summary_reflects_ifti_records` (which had explicitly documented the old shape as "a pre-existing formatting quirk... not something P28 is fixing here") to assert the new shape, and added `tests/test_reporting_dashboard_summary_shape_smoke.py` covering the contract, overdue/due-soon math, terminal-status exclusion, and the empty-org case.
**What live testing then found:** Verifying this fix required actually logging in through the real browser UI (per this session's own "test the feature in a browser before reporting complete" standard) rather than the pytest client or a hand-rolled Bearer-token curl check, both of which this whole session's testing has otherwise relied on exclusively. Doing that surfaced a much larger, previously-invisible bug: `GET /api/v1/reports/summary` (and `/ttr`, `/smr`) returned 401 "Missing or malformed Authorization header" for a real, freshly-logged-in browser session — even though the same session's `GET /api/v1/ifti/` succeeded. Root cause: `app/api/deps.py`'s `get_current_user` reads the `Authorization` header only; `app/api/routes/auth.py`'s `_current_user` (which `ifti.py` uses) reads the header **or** the httpOnly session cookie. `web/lib/auth.ts`'s `loginWithPassword()` never stores or attaches the returned access token as a header anywhere in the frontend — every authenticated `fetch()` call across the app relies solely on `credentials: 'include'` and the cookie. 32 of 49 route files (`customers.py`, `cases.py`, `dashboard.py`, `governance/controls.py`, `governance/policies.py`, `board_reporting.py`, `reports.py`, `monitoring.py`, `alerts.py`, `transactions.py`, `risk_assessment.py`, and 21 others) import the header-only dependency and are therefore unreachable from a real browser session; only the 17 files on the cookie-aware dependency work as-is. Confirmed directly: `curl -b <real session cookie>` against `governance/controls` and `board-reports` both return the same 401, ruling out this being specific to `reports.py`. `tests/conftest.py`'s `_auth()` helper (used by all 539 passing tests) authenticates via a Bearer header exclusively, so the entire test suite has zero coverage of the cookie-only path a real logged-in user actually takes — this is why 539 green tests never caught it. Not fixed in this pass — reconciling 32 files' auth wiring is real, security-sensitive, cross-cutting work of its own, parked as **P35** (see its own entry below) rather than folded silently into P30/P31's scope.
**Detail:** `app/services/reporting_service.py`'s `reporting_summary()`; `web/app/reporting/page.tsx`; `tests/test_reporting_dashboard_summary_shape_smoke.py`; `tests/test_ifti_dra_p28_workflow_smoke.py`.

### P35 — Most of the app was unreachable via a real browser session; fixed the frontend to send an Authorization header rather than broadening the backend's cookie acceptance
**Parked:** 2026-09-09, found live-testing P30/P31, as security-sensitive cross-cutting work needing an explicit approach decision rather than a rushed fix. **Resolved:** 2026-09-09, on explicit instruction, after you chose between the two options put to you.
**What it was:** `app/api/deps.py`'s `get_current_user` (used by 32 of 49 route files — `customers.py`, `cases.py`, `dashboard.py`, `governance/controls.py`, `governance/policies.py`, `board_reporting.py`, `reports.py`, `monitoring.py`, `alerts.py`, `transactions.py`, `risk_assessment.py`, and 21 others) reads the `Authorization` header only. `app/api/routes/auth.py`'s `_current_user` (used by the other 17, including `ifti.py`, `kyc.py`, `organisations.py`) reads the header *or* the httpOnly session cookie login sets. `web/lib/auth.ts`'s `loginWithPassword()`/`verifyMagicLink()` never stored or attached the returned `access_token` anywhere — every `fetch()` across the frontend relied solely on `credentials: 'include'` and the cookie, so the 32-file majority 401'd for every real logged-in browser session. `lib/signup.ts`'s `registerAccount()` (the actual "Start Free Trial" signup flow, a separate code path from `loginWithPassword()`) had the identical gap — the returned token was discarded there too, so the entire post-registration onboarding flow (`createOrganisation`, `selectIndustry`, `generateAmlProgram`, `createFirstCustomer`, etc.) was equally broken for a brand-new signup.
**Two ways to fix it, put to you as a decision:** (1) extend `app/api/deps.py`'s `get_current_user` to also accept the cookie, matching the pattern the other 17 files already use — smaller diff, but the session cookie is `SameSite=None` in production (required since the frontend and API are cross-origin) with **no CSRF protection anywhere in the codebase** (confirmed: no CSRF token, no custom-header check, no Origin/Referer validation in `app/middleware.py`) — `SameSite=None` cookies are attached by the browser on cross-site requests, so this would have silently broadened an already-present, unmitigated CSRF exposure from 17 files to essentially the whole product. (2) Fix the frontend to actually send the Authorization header it already receives from the backend on every login/register response — zero backend changes, no new CSRF surface (a header is never auto-attached by the browser cross-site, unlike a `SameSite=None` cookie), and every one of the 49 route files already accepts a Bearer header today regardless of which dependency it uses. **You chose (2).**
**What was done:** Added `apiFetch()` to `web/lib/auth.ts` — a drop-in `fetch()` replacement that attaches the stored token as `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (only when not already set), otherwise identical to `fetch()`. Token is stored in `sessionStorage` (not `localStorage`, to match its short lifetime and keep it out of the persistent user-metadata store), written by `loginWithPassword()`, `verifyMagicLink()`, and `registerAccount()` alongside their existing `storeUser()` calls (a new shared `storeSession()` helper covers all three). Replaced `fetch(` with `apiFetch(` at all ~140 authenticated call sites across 40 frontend files (every `credentials: "include"` call to the backend, found via `grep -rl "credentials:"`, plus a handful more found by cross-checking every call site against that list that lacked `credentials:` entirely and turned out to be authenticated staff pages missing it altogether — `app/governance/risk-matrix/page.tsx`, `app/industry/page.tsx`, and the notification-bell fetch in `Navbar.tsx`/`MobileNav.tsx`). Left genuinely public, token-in-URL endpoints (`app/verify/aml-program/[token]/page.tsx`, `components/Onboarding/CustomerPortal.tsx`, `app/onboarding/[token]/page.tsx`, `lib/pricing.ts`'s SSR-context billing-plans fetch) on plain `fetch()` — these are unauthenticated by design (an external auditor or a KYC applicant with a shared link, not a logged-in staff user) and correctly get no token even when one happens to be present. Four files (`retention.py`, `api-integrations`, `connectors`, `security` pages) already had their own locally-named `apiFetch()` convenience wrapper; aliased the import (`apiFetch as authFetch`) to avoid a naming collision and self-recursion, and pointed their wrapper's inner call at the real one. Also wired `Navbar.tsx`/`MobileNav.tsx`/`dashboard/page.tsx`'s sign-out buttons through a new `signOut()` helper that calls `POST /auth/logout` (server-side token revocation, via jti blacklist) before clearing local state — previously "sign out" only cleared client-side state and never actually revoked the token, a materially bigger gap once the token was doing real work client-side.
**Verified live** (not just pytest/tsc/eslint, given how this bug was found in the first place): registered a real user through the browser, logged in, and hit all 7 previously-401ing pages (`/dashboard`, `/customers`, `/governance/controls`, `/governance/policies`, `/governance/board-reports`, `/reporting`, `/monitoring`) — every `/api/v1/*` call returned 200. Separately ran the real "Start Free Trial" signup form end to end — registration succeeded, the token landed in storage, and the post-registration `/onboarding-setup` page's calls succeeded rather than 401ing.
**Not done:** Did not touch `app/api/deps.py` or `app/api/routes/auth.py` at all — no backend auth code changed. Did not add real CSRF protection (token or required custom header) for the 17 files that do still accept the cookie — that gap pre-dates this fix and pre-dates this session; flagging it here since P35's investigation is what surfaced it. Fixed immediately after, on instruction — see the CSRF entry below.
**Detail:** `web/lib/auth.ts` (`apiFetch`, `storeSession`, `signOut`, `getStoredToken`); `web/lib/signup.ts`'s `registerAccount()`; ~40 frontend files across `app/`, `components/`, `lib/` (every authenticated `fetch()` call site); `app/api/routes/auth.py`'s `POST /auth/logout` (already existed, now actually called).

### CSRF protection added for the 17 cookie-accepting route files (found investigating P35, fixed as its immediate follow-up)
**Parked:** never — found while investigating P35 (the auth-dependency split), fixed as your explicit immediate follow-up rather than left open.
**What it was:** `app/api/routes/auth.py`'s `_current_user` (used by 17 of 49 route files, plus `auth.py`'s own routes) accepts the session cookie alone with no CSRF mitigation anywhere in the codebase. That cookie is `SameSite=None` in production (required since the frontend and API are cross-origin, per `set_session_cookie()`'s own comment), and `SameSite=None` cookies are attached by the browser on cross-site requests — so a forged request from a malicious page, made while a victim is logged in, would have the cookie auto-attached and been treated as authenticated, for any of those 17 files' state-changing endpoints.
**What was done:** Standard double-submit cookie. `login`/`register` now also set a second, non-`httpOnly` `tvg_csrf` cookie (`set_csrf_cookie()`, mirroring `set_session_cookie()`) alongside the session cookie. `auth.py`'s `_decode_current_user()` — the single shared function behind `_current_user`/`_current_user_mfa_pending`, so this covers all 17 files without touching them individually — now tracks whether the request authenticated via the header or the cookie; for a cookie-authenticated state-changing request (`POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE`), it requires an `X-CSRF-Token` header matching the `tvg_csrf` cookie value (`secrets.compare_digest`, constant-time), rejecting with 403 otherwise. A request authenticating via the `Authorization` header (what the frontend always sends post-P35) skips the check entirely, since a header can't be attached by a forged cross-site request in the first place. `GET`s are exempt (assumed side-effect-free). Added `X-CSRF-Token` to the CORS `allow_headers` list (`app/main.py`) so the frontend can actually send it. On the frontend, `apiFetch()` now also reads the `tvg_csrf` cookie (via `document.cookie` — it's deliberately not `httpOnly`) and echoes it back on mutating requests, as a harmless belt-and-suspenders measure for the fallback case where no Bearer token is in storage yet; the frontend's own real traffic never actually needs this, since it always sends the header. Switched the stored access token from `sessionStorage` to `localStorage` in the same change — `sessionStorage` is per-tab, and a freshly opened tab with no token yet would otherwise fall back to cookie-only auth and get rejected by the new CSRF check on its first mutation; `localStorage` matches the token's existing `ACCESS_TOKEN_EXPIRY_MINUTES`-based lifetime (same exposure window the cookie already had) while being available to every tab. Also fixed a small pre-existing bug found in the same function: `clear_session_cookie`/`clear_csrf_cookie` were imported into `/auth/logout` but never actually called — the browser kept sending the (blacklisted, so inert) cookies until they naturally expired; both are now cleared on logout.
**Verified:** 7 new regression tests (`tests/test_csrf_double_submit_smoke.py`) — cookie-only mutation without the header rejected (403), with the wrong token rejected, with the correct token accepted, GET exempt, Bearer-header auth skips the check entirely (matching all 546 other passing tests), logout clears both cookies. Live-checked against a running server: registered via `curl`, confirmed a cookie-only `PATCH /auth/me` 403s without the token and 200s with it. In a real browser: confirmed the normal login → mutate flow (real `Authorization` header, from `localStorage`) still succeeds, and separately reproduced a forged-request scenario from within the page's own JS context (cookie sent automatically, no `Authorization` header, no CSRF header) and confirmed it's rejected with 403.
**Detail:** `app/services/auth_service.py` (`set_csrf_cookie`, `clear_csrf_cookie`, `new_csrf_token`); `app/api/routes/auth.py`'s `_decode_current_user()`, `register()`, `login()`, `logout()`; `app/config.py`'s `csrf_cookie_name`; `app/main.py`'s CORS `allow_headers`; `web/lib/auth.ts`'s `apiFetch()`, `getStoredToken()`/`storeToken()`/`clearToken()`; `tests/test_csrf_double_submit_smoke.py`.

### P33 — Re-triaged and fixed the ~28 route files P29 didn't name; found two more that were missed entirely
**Parked:** 2026-09-09 (Stage 11), pending re-triage of the "commercial/infrastructure, out of scope" bucket. **Resolved:** 2026-09-09, on explicit instruction.
**Re-triage confirmed the six files already flagged as AML/CTF-relevant on closer inspection** (`risk_assessment.py`, `aml_program.py`, `professional_assessment.py`, `rule_builder.py`, `examination_packs.py`, `customer_workflow.py`) **and found two more the original P29 pass missed entirely:** `recommendations.py` (acting on or dismissing a system-generated regulatory recommendation — its own docstring already claimed "Dismissal is auditable", but nothing wrote it anywhere) and `risk_matrix_config.py` (compliance officers customising the org's risk-factor weights and profile thresholds — core risk-scoring configuration). The remaining ~21 files (`billing.py`, `marketplace.py`, `connectors.py`, `health.py`, `branding.py`, `api_keys.py`, `storage.py`, `tenants.py`, `notifications.py`, `dashboard.py`, `analytics.py`, `benchmark.py`, `verify.py`, `security_monitor.py`, `customer_portal_public.py`, `customer_portal_staff.py`, `org_config.py`, `reporting_groups.py`, `tasks.py`, `training_triggers.py`, `ifti_e.py`, `ifti_receipts.py`, `integrations.py`) were re-confirmed genuinely out of scope — commercial/infrastructure, or (like `integrations.py`, a third-party data-provider connector) not itself an AML/CTF decision.
**Several of the eight files already had some record-keeping, just not centralised:** `OrgRiskMatrixVersion` snapshots (`risk_matrix_config.py`), `RiskScoreHistory` (`risk_assessment.py`), `CustomerWorkflowEvent` (`customer_workflow.py`), and direct `AuditLog` writes on a few endpoints (`create_rule`/`update_rule`/`delete_rule` in `rule_builder.py`; `create_assessment`/`submit_assessment`/`approve_assessment` in `risk_assessment.py`; `run_risk_assessment`/`approve_edd`/`final_decision` in `customer_workflow.py`) — the direct `AuditLog` writes use `app.models.audit_log` (confirmed already merged into `GET /audit/` by `test_audit_dual_table_smoke.py`), so those were left untouched; the domain-specific snapshot/history models were left in place (still useful for their own purpose) but now also write to the central trail alongside them.
**What was done:** Added `audit_service.log_action()` calls across all 8 files — 66 mutating endpoints in total. Where a file already funnels every state change through one shared helper (`risk_matrix_config.py`'s `_record_version()`, `customer_workflow.py`'s `_transition()`), the central-trail call was added inside that helper once, covering every action that flows through it rather than touching each endpoint individually. One endpoint was deliberately left unaudited: `rule_builder.py`'s `test_rule()` — its own docstring confirms it "never executes actions and never writes... a pure evaluation test", genuinely side-effect-free despite the `POST` verb. `risk_assessment.py`'s `recalculate_scores()` was also left alone — a derived re-computation from already-scored (and already-audited) factors, not an independent decision.
**Found along the way, not fixed (out of scope for an audit-trail pass):** `risk_assessment.py`'s `create_mitigation_library_item()` returns `{}` over real HTTP instead of the created item's fields — parked as **P36** above.
**Verified:** 8 new regression tests (`tests/test_p33_audit_coverage_sweep_smoke.py`), one representative flow per file, each confirming the action reaches `GET /audit/?entity_type=...&entity_id=...`. Full suite: 554 passed, mypy clean, ruff clean.
**Detail:** `app/api/routes/risk_assessment.py`, `aml_program.py`, `professional_assessment.py`, `rule_builder.py`, `examination_packs.py`, `customer_workflow.py`, `recommendations.py`, `risk_matrix_config.py`; `tests/test_p33_audit_coverage_sweep_smoke.py`.

### P12 — Onboarding wizard's "program" step now shows the real seeded AML program document
**Parked:** 2026-09-08 (Stage 7), as a monetisation/UX decision, not a bug fix. **Resolved:** 2026-09-09, per your explicit choice ("Show the real program" / "Drop it for now" on the paywall question).
**What changed:** `web/components/OnboardingWizard.tsx`'s `'program'` step now fetches and displays the org's real, auto-seeded `AMLProgram` document (`GET /api/v1/aml-program/versions`, taking the most recent version) instead of the separate `AmlProgramRecord` checklist with locked items and an upgrade prompt. The step shows section-completion progress (e.g. "18 of 22 program sections drafted from your industry's compliance pack") and a handful of named section highlights instead.
**Why `/versions` and not `GET /aml-program`:** the latter only returns a program with `status == active`, and a freshly-seeded program is still an unreviewed `draft` at this exact point in onboarding — nobody has activated it yet. `/versions` returns every version regardless of status, newest first, so the most recent one is genuinely the org's just-seeded document. Confirmed live: a fresh org's seeded program came back with 18 of 22 sections already populated from the remittance industry template.
**What did NOT change:** `generateAmlProgram()` (the `AmlProgramRecord` system) still runs in the background at the same point in the wizard, so the existing, unrelated `/aml-program` page (version history, program-health scoring, export-with-reason audit trail, QR-code public verification) keeps working exactly as before for every org — that page and its paywall are a real, separately-built product surface with no equivalent in the document system, and were out of scope for this decision. Only what the *wizard step itself* displays changed.
**Verified:** live end-to-end against a running backend — registered an org, selected an industry, set a risk profile, confirmed `GET /aml-program/versions` returns the populated document; `tsc`, eslint, and a full frontend production build all clean.
**Detail:** `web/components/OnboardingWizard.tsx`; `web/lib/signup.ts`'s `getLatestAmlProgramDocument()`; `app/api/routes/aml_program.py`'s `list_program_versions()` (unchanged, pre-existing endpoint).
