### P37 — Found live while verifying P12/P25: new organisation registration can fail with a 500 due to a database enum-name collision
**Found:** 2026-09-09, while running a live end-to-end smoke test of the P12 wizard change against a fresh database built from this repo's current migrations. **Status:** Parked 2026-09-10, per your direction. A production check found the frontend (Vercel) and backend (`api.veri-go.com.au`) both healthy, but didn't complete a live registration test — so whether production's `controlstatus` type actually has the wrong values is still unconfirmed. Not treated as urgent without a confirmed production failure; worth a quick `SELECT enum_range(NULL::controlstatus);` check next time you're in the Railway console, and definitely worth fixing before ever rebuilding production from these migrations from scratch. Not yet fixed — needs your go-ahead when picked back up, since fixing it means a new migration touching a live column.
**What:** Two unrelated SQLAlchemy models each define their own, differently-valued `ControlStatus` enum, and neither column gives it an explicit database type name:
- `app/models/aml_solution.py:65` (the legacy `Control.status` column) — values `effective` / `partially_effective` / `ineffective` / `not_tested`.
- `app/models/governance_controls.py:118` (`GovernanceControl.status`, the live one) — values `active` / `inactive` / `under_review` / `remediation` / `suspended`.

Because neither `Enum(ControlStatus)` column passes `name=`, SQLAlchemy derives the same Postgres type name (`controlstatus`) for both. Whichever model's migration creates that type first "wins" — and on a fresh database built from this repo's current migration chain, that's `aml_solution.py`'s version. Every new organisation signup calls `org_service.py`'s `_seed_aml_solution_and_risk_framework()`, which seeds real `GovernanceControl` rows with `status=ControlStatus.active` (the *other* enum's value) — and `"active"` isn't a valid value in the `controlstatus` type as actually created, so the insert throws `psycopg2.errors.InvalidTextRepresentation` and registration 500s.
**Reproduced concretely:** ran `alembic upgrade head` against an empty PostgreSQL 16 database using this repo's current migration chain, then called `POST /api/v1/auth/register` — got a 500, traced to exactly this insert. Worked around it for the rest of this session's live testing by manually `ALTER TYPE controlstatus ADD VALUE`-ing the missing values directly in the throwaway test database — **that workaround was applied only to a local, disposable test database for this session, not to any real database, and is not a fix.**
**Why this needs your go-ahead rather than being fixed now:** a real fix needs a new Alembic migration — giving one of the two columns (most likely `governance_controls.py`'s, since it's the live one) its own explicit, distinct Postgres enum type name, then migrating the column over. That's a schema change to a live table, which this session's standing instructions say needs sign-off, and it's outside what P12/P25 asked for.
**Why this is flagged as urgent rather than just parked:** if the real production database was built from the same migration chain (rather than having organically accumulated a `controlstatus` type with the right values from a different history), **new customer signups could be failing in production right now.** Worth checking directly — try registering a brand-new organisation against production, or query production's `controlstatus` enum values (`SELECT enum_range(NULL::controlstatus)`) and compare against `governance_controls.py`'s `ControlStatus` — before anything else on this list.
**Detail:** `app/models/aml_solution.py:65,414`; `app/models/governance_controls.py:118,261`; `app/services/org_service.py`'s `_seed_aml_solution_and_risk_framework()`.

## Resolved on your direction (2026-09-10, Stage 13 — SaaS Commercialisation)

### Stage 13 usage-limit enforcement — `PLAN_CATALOGUE`'s customer/user/org-count caps were defined and marketed but never enforced
**Found:** 2026-09-10, via a background investigation agent asked to verify Stage 13's status rather than take `CURRENT_STATE.md`'s one-line "Real, implemented" claim at face value. It confirmed Stripe billing, the plan catalogue, the billing dashboard, and multi-org RBAC are all genuinely real — but found `PLAN_CATALOGUE[plan]["limits"]` (customers/users/api_calls_month) was read nowhere outside the pricing/billing-info display, so every org on every plan, including free-trial, could onboard unlimited customers, add unlimited team members, and an owner could spin up unlimited additional organisations. It also found the pricing page's Enterprise-tier "Up to 2 compliance packs" claim had zero backend support — no model or field limits an org to any number of industry packs.
**Resolved:** 2026-09-10, per your direction ("Remove the claim from pricing copy" / "Yes, build all three").
**What changed:**
- `app/models/billing.py`: added `FREE_TRIAL_LIMITS = {"customers": 10, "users": 1, "api_calls_month": 250}` — the implicit cap for orgs with no `PLAN_CATALOGUE` subscription row (matching the pricing page's existing "Up to 10 customers" trial copy). Also fixed stale `BillingPlan` enum price comments left over from an old pricing round.
- `app/services/billing_service.py`: added `_plan_limits()` (looks up the caller's current plan's limits, falling back to `FREE_TRIAL_LIMITS`) and three enforcement functions — `enforce_customer_limit()`, `enforce_user_limit()` (both `-1` = unlimited, raise `HTTPException(403)` with an upgrade-prompt message at the cap), and `enforce_org_creation_limit()` (free-trial accounts capped at 1 organisation; the cap lifts once *any* organisation the user owns is on a paid plan, since no paid tier's own description promises a further multi-org limit to enforce).
- Wired in at the three creation points: `enforce_customer_limit()` in `app/api/routes/customers.py::create_customer()`; `enforce_user_limit()` in `app/api/routes/organisations.py::add_member()`; `enforce_org_creation_limit()` in `app/api/routes/organisations.py::create()`.
- `web/app/pricing/page.tsx`: removed the unbacked "Up to 2 compliance packs" claim — Enterprise tier and the comparison table now both say "1 industry compliance pack", matching every other tier and what the product actually does.
**What did NOT change / known scope limits (disclosed, not fixed this round):**
- **Customer-limit enforcement only covers the single-customer creation endpoint.** `app/api/routes/customers.py::bulk_import_customers()` (line ~2145) and `app/services/onboarding_service.py`'s two direct `Customer(...)` construction sites (onboarding-wizard "first customer" flow) both create `Customer` rows without going through `enforce_customer_limit()` — an org could still exceed its plan's customer cap via bulk import or onboarding. Confirmed by grep, not yet fixed.
- **`api_calls_month` is defined in every plan's `limits` dict and read by `_plan_limits()`, but nothing enforces it** — no request-counting middleware or per-org API-call counter exists anywhere in the codebase. Only `customers`, `users`, and the free-trial org-count were scoped and built this round, per your "build all three" answer (which was asked about exactly these three, not `api_calls_month`).
- Stage 13 as a whole is **not** fully complete: the broader "industry compliance packs as a real, separately-metered paid feature" and further account-management build-out from the original Stage 13 brief were not requested this round and remain open. *(Both are now resolved — see "Stage 13's remaining item" in Resolved below, 2026-09-14.)*
**Verified:** 93 tests across every billing/customer/onboarding/organisation-touching test file pass (including 6 new tests in `tests/test_billing_usage_limits_smoke.py` covering all three limits, both "blocked at the free-trial cap" and "succeeds after upgrading"). One pre-existing test (`test_org_members_n_plus_one_smoke.py`) assumed unlimited org members and needed updating to upgrade its test org's plan first — a real, expected interaction with the new limit, not a bug. mypy and ruff (check + format) clean on all 6 changed/added files.
**Detail:** `app/models/billing.py`; `app/services/billing_service.py`; `app/api/routes/customers.py`; `app/api/routes/organisations.py`; `web/app/pricing/page.tsx`; `tests/test_billing_usage_limits_smoke.py`; `tests/test_org_members_n_plus_one_smoke.py`.

### P39 — Customer-limit enforcement now also covers bulk import and the onboarding wizard's own customer-creation paths
**Found & parked:** 2026-09-10, disclosed as a known scope limit in the Stage 13 usage-limit entry above. **Resolved:** 2026-09-10, per your direction ("pick up 39 40").
**What changed:** `billing_service.py` gained `remaining_customer_capacity()` (returns `None` for unlimited, else the exact number of customers still free to create), refactored out of `enforce_customer_limit()` via a shared `_customer_capacity()` helper. Three more real customer-creation sites now respect it:
- `onboarding_service.py::create_session()` — always creates a draft `Customer` immediately (so ops-entered applicants have somewhere to attach documents), called by both `POST /onboarding/sessions` and, in a loop, `bulk_create_sessions()` (`POST /onboarding/import/csv|excel`). Now calls `enforce_customer_limit()` before any writes; in the bulk-invite loop, a blocked row lands in that row's own `errors` entry without failing the rest of the batch (existing per-row try/except behaviour, unchanged).
- `onboarding_service.py::submit_onboarding()`'s fallback branch (creates a `Customer` only if `session.customer_id` wasn't already set — a rare/legacy path per its own docstring) — same check added.
- `customers.py::bulk_import_customers()` (CSV/Excel customer import) — computes remaining capacity once before the row loop, decrements it per customer actually created, and once it hits zero, the rest of the rows land in the existing `skipped_rows` list with a "customer limit reached" reason rather than silently exceeding the cap or aborting the whole upload.
**Verified:** 4 new tests in `tests/test_customer_limit_onboarding_and_bulk_import.py` (session creation blocked at cap / succeeds under it; bulk CSV onboarding invite reports a per-row limit error without failing the batch; bulk customer CSV import creates up to the remaining slots and skips the rest with the limit reason). Full 97-test targeted regression pass (billing/customer/onboarding/organisation surfaces) still green; mypy and ruff clean.

### P40 — `api_calls_month` is now enforced, on a real API-key auth path built for it
**Parked:** 2026-09-10, in the Stage 13 entry above, as "needs a counting-strategy decision." **Resolved:** 2026-09-10, per your direction across two questions — first the counting mechanism ("DB-backed counter"), then, after a deeper investigation surfaced a bigger blocker mid-implementation, the scope question it forced ("build real API-key auth first").
**What the investigation found before writing any code:** `app/services/api_key_service.py::authenticate_api_key()` — hash lookup, active/expiry check, `last_used_at` update — already existed and was fully correct, but was **never called from anywhere**. No route accepted an API key to authenticate a request; API keys could only be created/listed/revoked, never actually used. Every request, including everything the web app's own browser session makes, went through the same JWT auth. Meter­ing *that* against `api_calls_month` (250/month on free trial) would have exhausted the cap within minutes of ordinary dashboard use and broken the product — so the real blocker wasn't "how to count," it was "count what," and building the DB counter first would have been building on a target that didn't exist yet.
**What changed:**
- `app/api/deps.py::get_current_user()` — the shared dependency behind `require_analyst_or_above`/`require_compliance_or_above`/etc., used by 29 of the app's ~46 route files — now accepts either credential: `Authorization: Bearer <jwt>` (checked first, unmetered, zero behaviour change) or, only when no Bearer token is present, `X-API-Key: <key>` via `authenticate_api_key()`, resolving to the same `User` object so every route downstream works identically regardless of which credential was used.
- `app/models/billing.py` — new `ApiUsageCounter` model (`org_id`, `period` = "YYYY-MM", `count`), one row per org per calendar month (no scheduled reset job needed — a new month just starts a new row). New Alembic migration `d472643f4d3b_api_usage_counters.py`, guarded the same way as other post-baseline migrations (no-ops if `create_all()` already made the table).
- `app/services/billing_service.py::record_api_call()` — increments the current month's counter and raises `HTTPException(429, ...)` once it exceeds the plan's `api_calls_month` limit. Called **only** from `get_current_user()`'s `X-API-Key` branch — ordinary JWT session traffic, however much of it there is, is never counted or capped.
**What did NOT change / known scope limits (disclosed, not fixed this round):** `app/api/routes/auth.py` has its own parallel, separate auth-dependency chain (`_current_user`/`_decode_current_user`/`_require_roles`), used by 17 route files (including `onboarding.py`) — a pre-existing duplication in the codebase, not something introduced here. That path is still JWT-only; an API key won't authenticate against routes that exclusively depend on it. Full unification of the two auth-dependency chains was out of scope for this round.
**Verified:** 5 new tests in `tests/test_api_key_auth_and_usage_limit.py` — API key authenticates real requests; invalid key and missing credentials both still 401; JWT calls (5 of them) leave `ApiUsageCounter` untouched while the same number of API-key calls create it with the right count; a plan's cap blocks the call that would exceed it (429, correct message) while the call exactly at the cap still succeeds. Given the breadth of what `get_current_user()` touches, ran the **entire backend suite** (not just a targeted subset) as the regression check: 569 passed, 0 failures. Migration applied and downgraded cleanly against a real Postgres database. mypy and ruff (check + format) clean on all 5 changed/added files.
**Detail:** `app/api/deps.py`; `app/models/billing.py`; `app/services/billing_service.py`; `alembic/versions/d472643f4d3b_api_usage_counters.py`; `tests/test_api_key_auth_and_usage_limit.py`.

### P41 — Sanctions/PEP screening no longer hardcoded to always return "clear"
**Found:** 2026-09-10, during a Stage 14 (External Integrations) investigation. **Fixed same day**, ahead of the rest of Stage 14, per your direction ("Fix now, ahead of the rest of Stage 14") — flagged as a live AML/CTF correctness bug rather than an ordinary feature gap, since a sanctions screen that can never find a match is closer in severity to P37 than a missing integration.
**What it was:** `POST /screening/run` — the real, audit-logged, alert-raising screening endpoint — called `_simulate_screening()`, which was **hardcoded to always return `status=clear, match_count=0` regardless of the name screened**; its own docstring said "Currently runs in simulation mode — results are placeholders." The same function backed `/screening/quick-screen`'s PEP branch, `/screening/{id}/re-screen`, and `/screening/batch`. Separately, the onboarding portal's own early sanctions check (`onboarding_service.py::submit_onboarding`, via `sanctions_screening.py::screen_name()`) matched only against a hardcoded 3-entry fake watchlist ("John Doe Sanction", "Jane Criminal", "Acme Shell Corp"). A real, working provider architecture already existed for both (`app.integrations.sanctions` — `InternalSanctionsProvider` + a working `ComplyAdvantageProvider`; `app.integrations.pep` — `StubPEPProvider` + a working ComplyAdvantage adapter) but **nothing in the app called either factory**.
**What changed:**
- `app/api/routes/screening.py` — new `_run_real_screening()`/`_screen()` route sanctions (`sanctions`/`ubo_sanctions`) and PEP (`pep`/`ubo_pep`) screening types through `app.integrations.sanctions`/`app.integrations.pep`'s real provider factories (selected by the existing `SANCTIONS_PROVIDER`/`PEP_PROVIDER` settings), converting their `SanctionsResult`/`PEPResult` into the same dict shape `_simulate_screening()` used, so every downstream consumer (`ScreeningRecord`, alert-raising) is unchanged. `_simulate_screening()` itself is kept, now used only for screening types with no real provider layer (watchlist, adverse_media, regulatory, law_enforcement, ubo_adverse, manual_review — see P44). All four route handlers (`run_screening`, `quick_screen`, `re_screen`, `batch_screen`) became `async def` to await the real provider calls, mirroring the existing `screen_crypto_wallet` route's pattern (including its `try/except (NotImplementedError, ProviderUnavailableError)` fallback-to-simulation for an unconfigured/unimplemented provider like `worldcheck`).
- `app/services/sanctions_screening.py` — rewritten: `screen_name()`/`screen_transaction()` now delegate to `app.integrations.sanctions.get_provider()` instead of matching a fixed local list; both became `async def`. The old `SAMPLE_WATCHLIST` and its bespoke `difflib`-based matching were removed (the real provider already does its own matching).
- `app/services/onboarding_service.py::submit_onboarding()` and `app/api/routes/onboarding.py::portal_final_submit` became `async def` to await the now-async `screen_name()`.
**What did NOT change / known scope limits (disclosed, not silently fixed):**
- **`InternalSanctionsProvider`'s default list data is minimal** — 3 real DFAT entries (Al-Qaeda, Islamic State, Taliban), empty OFAC/UN/EU lists. The routing bug is fixed (screening now genuinely runs against the input name instead of ignoring it), but real production-grade sanctions coverage needs either full published list data or a paid provider — tracked as **P42**.
- **PEP screening's default behaviour is unchanged** — `StubPEPProvider` still always returns "not a PEP" (there's no free public PEP dataset the way there is for sanctions lists); what's fixed is that it's now real, config-driven code instead of a hardcoded bypass. Flipping `PEP_PROVIDER=complyadvantage` + a key gets real PEP screening immediately. Tracked as **P43**.
- The rest of the Stage 14 investigation's findings (identity/KYC default, crypto's silent simulation fallback, the decorative Integration Hub marketplace, webhooks that can never fire) were tracked as P44 — since renamed **P45** now that the crypto fallback and dead webhook dispatcher are resolved (see below), leaving only the identity/KYC default and the decorative marketplace open under that ID.
**Verified:** 6 new tests in `tests/test_sanctions_pep_screening_real_provider.py` — a real DFAT name (`"Al-Qaeda"`) now returns `potential_match` and raises a `ScreeningAlert`; an unrelated name stays `clear`; quick-screen's sanctions branch uses the real provider; PEP routing proven real by monkeypatching in a fake matching provider (same technique `test_crypto_wallet_screening.py` already uses for crypto) and confirming the match surfaces end-to-end; default PEP behaviour confirmed still clear (no regression); onboarding's `submit_onboarding()` now flags a real sanctions match on the applicant's own name. 3 existing tests updated for the async signature change and to use real DFAT names instead of the removed fake watchlist (`test_coverage_boost.py`, `test_onboarding_submit_creates_draft_customer.py`, `test_onboarding_wizard_data_reaches_customer.py`). Given the breadth of files touched, ran the full backend suite: 575 passed, 0 failures. mypy and ruff (check + format) clean on all 8 changed/added files.
**Detail:** `app/api/routes/screening.py`; `app/services/sanctions_screening.py`; `app/services/onboarding_service.py`; `app/api/routes/onboarding.py`; `tests/test_sanctions_pep_screening_real_provider.py`; `tests/test_coverage_boost.py`; `tests/test_onboarding_submit_creates_draft_customer.py`; `tests/test_onboarding_wizard_data_reaches_customer.py`.

### Crypto wallet screening's default provider now real (no more silent simulation fallback); webhook dispatch now wired to real events
**Found:** 2026-09-10, in the Stage 14 investigation, tracked as part of P44 (since split — the marketplace/identity-default items are now **P45**). **Resolved same day**, per your direction ("P42–P44").
**What changed:**
- `app/integrations/crypto/factory.py::get_provider()` — the unregistered default (`crypto_provider="internal"`, or any unrecognised value) previously fell through to `raise NotImplementedError`, which `screen_crypto_wallet` caught and silently treated as "no provider configured," degrading to simulation without surfacing that anywhere. It now defaults to `OFACSDNProvider` — the same free, no-API-key, self-hosted OFAC sanctioned-address list already used elsewhere in this module (fetches live from a GitHub-mirrored list, 6h in-process cache) — so an unconfigured org gets real crypto sanctions screening, not a placeholder. `trm_labs` (genuinely not implemented) is the only case that still falls through to simulation, and only once an org has purchased the enterprise add-on that gates it (checked earlier in the route, before the factory is ever called).
- `app/services/api_key_service.py` — new `dispatch_event_background()`: `dispatch_event()`/`fire_webhook()` were already real (genuine HMAC-signed `httpx.post()` delivery) but made a **blocking** network call with a 10s timeout per matching endpoint — calling it inline from a request handler would let a slow or unreachable customer-configured webhook URL stall that request. `dispatch_event_background()` is a `FastAPI BackgroundTasks`-safe wrapper (runs after the response is already sent) that opens its own DB session via `app.worker.add_background_task()` — the existing, previously-unused helper built for exactly this pattern.
- Wired at two real event sources: `customers.py::create_customer()` fires `customer.created`; `screening.py::run_screening()` fires `aml_alert.created` for each `ScreeningAlert` actually raised. Both fire only after `db.commit()`, so the data a subscriber's webhook receiver might query back already exists.
**What did NOT change:** every other cataloged `WebhookEvent` (`kyc.status_changed`, `report.submitted`, `report.approved`, `case.assigned`, `case.escalated`, `customer.risk_changed`, `transaction.flagged`) is still never dispatched — only the two wired this round fire. The decorative Integration Hub marketplace and identity/KYC's default provider are untouched (P45).
**Verified:** `test_get_crypto_provider_defaults_to_ofac_sdn_when_internal` (factory-level) and `test_crypto_wallet_route_defaults_to_real_ofac_sdn_provider` (route-level, monkeypatched `httpx.AsyncClient.get` to avoid a real network call) confirm the new default; the pre-existing "falls back to simulation when unconfigured" test was rewritten (`test_crypto_wallet_route_falls_back_to_simulation_when_provider_not_implemented`) since that scenario now requires an actually-unimplemented provider, not just an unconfigured one. 5 new tests in `tests/test_webhook_dispatch_on_real_events.py`: route-level tests confirm `create_customer`/`run_screening` call `dispatch_event_background` with the right event/payload/org only when appropriate (not on a clear screening result); isolated tests of `dispatch_event_background` itself (using a session bound directly to the test engine, since a background task's fresh `SessionLocal()` never sees this test suite's per-test nested transaction) confirm it actually delivers to a real `WebhookEndpoint` and swallows delivery exceptions rather than letting them propagate. Full backend suite: 580 passed. mypy and ruff clean on all 6 changed/added files.
**Detail:** `app/integrations/crypto/factory.py`; `app/services/api_key_service.py`; `app/api/routes/customers.py`; `app/api/routes/screening.py`; `tests/test_crypto_wallet_screening.py`; `tests/test_webhook_dispatch_on_real_events.py`.

### P46 — `GET /aml-program?include_sections=true` leaked the full real program to any plan, including free trial
**Found:** 2026-09-11, while acting on the monetisation playbook's free-trial-leakage action item. Set out to "watermark the export," but a code investigation first found **there is no file export anywhere in this codebase at all** — no PDF/DOCX generation exists (repo-wide search for weasyprint/reportlab/wkhtmltopdf/python-docx/pdfkit/xhtml2pdf: zero hits), and the existing "Export" button on `/aml-program` (`web/app/aml-program/page.tsx`) just re-fetches the same JSON via an audit-logged endpoint and shows a confirmation message — nothing is ever actually saved as a file. So there was nothing to watermark. Looking for the real equivalent gap instead found this: `app/api/routes/aml_program.py`'s `GET /aml-program` and `GET /aml-program/{id}`, called with `?include_sections=true`, returned **every section's full narrative text** — CDD procedures, transaction monitoring, sanctions/SMR/TTR/IFTI procedures, the entire usable program — to any analyst+ role user (which every registered user is by default) with **no billing/plan check at all**. The sibling AML program system (`AmlProgramRecord`, `organisations.py`'s `_program_response`) already correctly gates its own full-content response behind `billing_service.is_feature_enabled(plan, "full_aml_program")`; this endpoint had no equivalent check — an inconsistency between the two systems, not a deliberate design choice. Not currently exposed through any UI button (`include_sections=true` is never sent by any frontend page today), but directly callable by any authenticated user via the API with zero extra privilege.
**Resolved:** same day, per your direction ("proceed next action item").
**What changed:** Added `_has_full_program_access()` (reuses the existing `billing_service.current_plan()`/`is_feature_enabled(..., "full_aml_program")` primitive — no new billing infrastructure) and a `_PREVIEW_SECTIONS = {"overview", "scope"}` constant to `aml_program.py`. `_program_dict()` now takes a `full_access` flag: when `include_sections=true` and the org's plan doesn't have `full_aml_program`, every section outside the preview set is redacted to `null`, with `is_preview: true` and a `locked_sections` list added — same shape/spirit as `AmlProgramRecord`'s existing item-level preview redaction. Both `GET /aml-program` and `GET /aml-program/{program_id}` now compute and pass this flag. `GET /aml-program/versions` (what the onboarding wizard actually reads) was already safe — it never requests `include_sections`, so no change was needed there.
**What did NOT change:** no new billing concept, no export/watermarking system was built (there's nothing to build one for yet — see P44/P45 for the still-decorative Integration Hub, and note a real file-export feature doesn't exist for either AML program system). If a real PDF/DOCX export is ever added, it will need this same `full_aml_program` check wired into it explicitly, not inherit this fix automatically.
**Verified:** 4 new tests in `tests/test_aml_program_preview_gating_smoke.py` — free-trial org sees `overview`/`scope` but `null` for `cdd_individuals`/`sanctions_procedures` with both in `locked_sections`; upgrading to `starter` reveals the real text; the default (no `include_sections`) response is unaffected either way (no regression); `GET /aml-program/{id}` gates identically. Full backend suite: 584 passed. mypy and ruff clean on both changed/added files.
**Detail:** `app/api/routes/aml_program.py`; `tests/test_aml_program_preview_gating_smoke.py`.

### PLAN_CATALOGUE repriced to Compliance/Scale/Enterprise; Annual Independent Review added as an AddonKey
**Requested:** 2026-09-11, per your direction ("do 1 & 2") after confirming the monetisation playbook's market-share-first pricing and dropping the exit-fee idea.
**What changed:**
- `app/models/billing.py`'s `PLAN_CATALOGUE` — `BillingPlan` enum members are unchanged (`starter`/`professional`/`enterprise`/`vvip`/`free_trial` stay the stable DB/API identifiers; only display names, pricing, and limits changed, so no migration was needed): `starter` is now **Compliance** ($299/mo, was Starter $59) — the market-entry tier, capped for scale (100 customers, 1-2 users) not capability (real screening, full unwatermarked program, live regulatory updates all included); `professional` is now **Scale** ($799/mo, was Professional $79) — 1,000 customers, 5 users, transaction monitoring/case management, webhooks/API access; `enterprise` stays **Enterprise** but repriced to $2,999/mo (was $299) as the custom negotiation anchor, now genuinely unlimited customers/users/API calls. `vvip` (custom, above Enterprise) is untouched. Annual prices set at "10 months for the price of 12" (a real discount, not the old ad-hoc numbers).
- `FEATURE_DEFINITIONS`/`DEFAULT_PLAN_FEATURES` — added `customers_100`/`customers_1000` feature codes for the new limits; the old `customers_500`/`customers_5000` codes are left defined (not deleted) since `seed_feature_catalog()` only inserts missing `(plan, feature_code)` toggle rows and never updates/removes existing ones — an environment that already seeded the old codes keeps them as harmless disabled rows rather than orphaned unknown codes on a fresh `DEFAULT_PLAN_FEATURES` read.
- New `AddonKey.independent_review` in `ADDON_CATALOGUE` — a human service (Verigo's dedicated review team, separate from the platform-build side), not a software feature, so `unlocks_providers: []`; price is `None` (TBA, per your direction, not yet set); `requires_plan` covers every paid tier (`starter`/`professional`/`enterprise`/`vvip`) per the playbook's "buyable by any paid tier" — the existing `purchase_addon`/`has_addon`/`cancel_addon`/`addon_catalogue` functions needed zero changes, since they were already generic over `AddonKey`.
- `web/app/pricing/page.tsx` — tier names/feature bullets/comparison-table numbers updated to match (dollar amounts already came from the backend via `fetchPlanPrices()` and needed no separate edit); fixed the amber AML/CTF Program disclaimer, which previously said every paid plan was "reference template only" — that's no longer true (P41/P46 made screening and full-content access real for paid plans this session), so it now correctly scopes that caveat to the Free Trial preview only.
**What did NOT change (known follow-up):** the pricing page's long feature-comparison grid (~9 groups, transaction monitoring/case management/IFTI/etc. true-false-by-tier) still reflects the old Starter/Professional capability split in most rows — only the plan names and the three numeric rows (customer/user/API-call limits) were corrected. Reconciling the full grid with Compliance's new "real screening + full program, capped for scale not capability" positioning is a real content/product-positioning pass, not a number sync, and wasn't done here.
**Verified:** 6 new tests (`test_billing_addons.py`) covering the new addon's catalogue entry, availability on every paid tier, and rejection on free trial. Existing tests using hardcoded old numbers were caught and fixed: `test_coverage_boost.py::test_plan_pricing_override` (asserted the old $59 default), `test_api_key_auth_and_usage_limit.py::test_api_key_calls_blocked_once_plan_cap_reached` (pre-seeded a counter assuming the old 1,000/month starter cap — real bug this repricing would have silently broken had it not been caught: the test now pre-seeds to the new 500 cap). Two stale explanatory comments (not assertions) fixed in `test_billing_usage_limits_smoke.py`. Full backend suite: 589 passed. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean on the pricing page. mypy and ruff clean on all changed Python files.
**Detail:** `app/models/billing.py`; `web/app/pricing/page.tsx`; `tests/test_billing_addons.py`; `tests/test_coverage_boost.py`; `tests/test_api_key_auth_and_usage_limit.py`; `tests/test_billing_usage_limits_smoke.py`.

### P42 — Self-hosted DFAT/OFAC/UN sanctions list ingestion ("build on free")
**Requested:** 2026-09-11, per your direction ("build on free") after a `WebSearch`-based research pass corrected several of this session's own earlier assumptions about which compliance data sources are actually free (OpenSanctions is not — commercial reseller licence, quote-based; DVS/identity verification is never free; email/SMS free tiers have largely disappeared industry-wide). Of the options surveyed, self-hosting the DFAT/OFAC/UN consolidated lists was the one clear, no-cost, no-decision-needed win, mirroring the pattern `app/integrations/crypto/ofac_sdn.py` already uses for crypto address screening.
**What changed:** `app/integrations/sanctions/internal.py`'s `InternalSanctionsProvider` no longer screens against 3 hardcoded names. It now has three real fetch-and-cache functions, each hitting an official free government source with no API key: OFAC SDN (`sanctionslistservice.ofac.treas.gov`'s `SDN.CSV`), the UN Security Council Consolidated List (`scsanctions.un.org`'s `consolidated.xml`), and the DFAT Consolidated List (`dfat.gov.au`'s `regulation8_consolidated.xlsx`, parsed with the already-installed `openpyxl`, locating the name column by its header text — "Name of Individual or Entity" — rather than a hardcoded index, so a column reorder on DFAT's end doesn't silently start reading the wrong field). Each list is cached in-process for 12 hours, same TTL-cache shape as `ofac_sdn.py`.
Live fetching is gated behind a new `settings.sanctions_live_lists_enabled` flag, **off by default**. Two reasons: (1) this session's sandbox has the same egress block against these exact government domains that blocked `WebFetch` from verifying them directly (confirmed live — `dfat.gov.au`, `ofac.treasury.gov`, and `un.org` all returned `EGRESS_BLOCKED`), so the exact download URLs were sourced from `WebSearch` result snippets and citations rather than a direct fetch, and deserve a real-traffic check before going live in an environment that can actually reach them; (2) `InternalSanctionsProvider` is the *default* sanctions provider, invoked incidentally by dozens of unrelated tests (screening, onboarding, webhook dispatch) — leaving live fetching on unconditionally would make broad swaths of the test suite dependent on three government websites' uptime and latency, a real reliability regression regardless of source correctness. When the flag is off, or when a fetch fails before any fetch has ever succeeded, `DFAT_AU` falls back to the same three well-known entries this module always shipped with (`Al-Qaeda`/`Islamic State`/`Taliban`) so screening never runs against zero data and every existing test that asserts on those names keeps working unchanged; `OFAC_SDN`/`UN_CONSOLIDATED` have no such seed (they were already empty before this change) and simply stay empty until enabled. A fetch failure *after* a previous success (flag on, but a source is temporarily down) serves the last good cached copy rather than dropping to empty.
**What did NOT change:** `EU_CONSOLIDATED` remains an empty, always-checked list — no free EU consolidated-sanctions source was identified in the research pass, so it wasn't built; this is a known, small residual gap, not silently dropped (the module docstring says so explicitly). P43 (no free PEP data source) is unaffected and still needs a paid-provider decision — nothing free exists there to build.
**Verified:** 8 new tests (`tests/test_sanctions_live_lists.py`), each mocking `httpx.AsyncClient.get` (same technique as `test_crypto_wallet_screening.py`'s `OFACSDNProvider` tests) so no real network call is made: each of the three fetchers correctly parses a realistic fixture of its format (headerless OFAC CSV, UN individuals+entities XML, DFAT xlsx with the name column found by header text); the live-lists-disabled default returns the DFAT seed / empty lists; enabling the flag fetches and caches (a second call within the TTL doesn't refetch); a first-ever fetch failure falls back to the seed; a later fetch failure after a prior success serves the last good cache instead of dropping data; and an end-to-end `InternalSanctionsProvider.screen()` call finds a live-fetched name and reports all four list names in `lists_checked`. Existing sanctions/webhook/crypto test files (150 tests) still pass unchanged, proving the default-off fallback preserves every prior hardcoded-match assumption. Full backend suite: 597 passed. mypy (matching CI's exact `mypy app/ --ignore-missing-imports --no-strict-optional` invocation) and ruff (check + format) clean on all changed/added files.
**Detail:** `app/integrations/sanctions/internal.py`; `app/config.py`; `tests/test_sanctions_live_lists.py`.

## Resolved on your direction (2026-09-11, "p37")

### P37 — `ControlStatus` enum-name collision fixed; new-org registration no longer breaks against a database built from this repo's migrations
**Found:** 2026-09-09, while running a live end-to-end smoke test of the P12 wizard change. Full original write-up further up this file. **Parked:** 2026-09-10, pending an inconclusive production check. **Resolved:** 2026-09-11, per your direction ("p37").
**What changed:**
- `app/models/governance_controls.py`'s `GovernanceControl.status` column and `app/models/aml_solution.py`'s `Control.status` column each now pass an explicit, distinct `name=` to their `Enum(ControlStatus)` — `"governance_control_status"` and `"legacy_control_status"` respectively — so SQLAlchemy no longer derives the same default Postgres type name (`controlstatus`) for both.
- New migration `alembic/versions/96e0b54fc153_split_controlstatus_enum_type.py`: for any *existing* database still carrying the old shared `controlstatus` type, creates the two new distinctly-named types with each model's real value set, casts both columns over (`USING status::text::<new type>`), then drops the old shared type. Idempotent both directions — on a database built fresh *after* this fix, the baseline migration's `Base.metadata.create_all()` already reads the corrected models and creates the two split types directly, so this migration correctly no-ops there (checked via `pg_type` existence, not a revision-number assumption) rather than erroring on "type already exists."
**Why this needed real verification, not just a code read:** the whole bug only exists in Postgres — this repo's own test suite runs on SQLite (`tests/conftest.py`), where `Enum()` is emulated per-column as a local CHECK constraint with no shared named type at all, so a normal pytest run can't see this class of bug either breaking or getting fixed. Verified instead against a real local PostgreSQL 16 database (`verigo_dev`, already carrying the pre-fix schema from this session's earlier P37 investigation): confirmed the pre-fix shared type only had `aml_solution.py`'s 4 values, migrated it, confirmed the post-fix split types have the right value sets with the existing 27 `governance_controls` rows (all `status='active'`) preserved intact, then downgraded and re-upgraded cleanly. Separately built a **brand-new, empty** Postgres 16 database and ran the full 21-migration chain from scratch against it (the actual disaster-recovery/fresh-environment scenario the original finding warned about) — confirmed no shared `controlstatus` type is ever created there at all (the split types come straight from the baseline), then called the real `POST /api/v1/auth/register` endpoint through the actual FastAPI app against that fresh database: **201, with `AML Solution seeded: ... controls=9` and 9 real `governance_controls` rows written with `status='active'`** — the exact insert the original finding predicted would 500.
**Why this is very likely to have been failing in production, not just "possibly":** the collision is deterministic — whichever of the two tables SQLAlchemy's metadata-sorted `create_all()` processes first wins the shared type's value set, and that ordering is a property of the model graph, not the environment. Any database built by running this repo's actual migration chain (the documented, intended path — the baseline migration's own docstring says so) gets the same losing order every time. This was flagged clearly rather than silently assumed fixed everywhere: **the actual production database was not touched or verified from this session** (no production credentials or access here) — recommend running `SELECT enum_range(NULL::controlstatus);` in the Railway console to confirm, then applying this migration there.
**What did NOT change / new finding surfaced:** fixing this one collision by hand (rather than, say, a codebase-wide sweep) is what led to grep-checking for other same-named Python enum classes across `app/models/*.py` — found the identical pattern 8 more times, one of them (`CustomerType`) confirmed as a second live, active bug blocking business-entity onboarding today. Not fixed in this pass — P37 was the scoped, requested item — tracked as new item **P47**, not silently dropped.
**Verified:** 5 new tests (`tests/test_p37_controlstatus_enum_collision_smoke.py`) — both columns carry their own distinct explicit type name; the two names no longer collide; the migration's hardcoded value tuples stay in sync with the real model enums (guards against silent drift if either `ControlStatus` is ever extended); the two enums are confirmed to have zero value overlap (documents why the original collision was a real bug, not cosmetic). Full backend suite: 602 passed. mypy (CI's exact invocation) and ruff (check + format) clean on all changed/added files. Live Postgres verification as described above — both the "existing broken database" and "genuinely fresh database" paths, including a real HTTP registration call, not just a migration dry-run.
**Detail:** `app/models/aml_solution.py`; `app/models/governance_controls.py`; `alembic/versions/96e0b54fc153_split_controlstatus_enum_type.py`; `tests/test_p37_controlstatus_enum_collision_smoke.py`.

## Resolved on your direction (2026-09-11, "yes take all in as one batch")

### P47 — All 8 remaining enum type-name collisions split, same fix as P37, applied in one batch
**Found:** 2026-09-11, grep-sweeping for other same-named Python enum classes while fixing P37. Full original write-up further up this file. **Resolved:** 2026-09-11, per your direction to take the whole batch rather than one at a time.
**What changed:** every column identified in the P37-adjacent sweep now passes an explicit, distinct `name=` to its `Enum(...)`, mirroring exactly what P37 did for `ControlStatus` — 9 columns across 8 files:
- `AlertStatus`: `monitoring.py`'s `TransactionAlert.status` → `transaction_alert_status`; `screening.py`'s `ScreeningAlert.status` and `AdverseMediaResult.review_status` (same enum, two columns) → `screening_alert_status`.
- `AssessmentStatus` (three-way): `aml_solution.py`'s `RiskAssessment.status` → `legacy_risk_assessment_status`; `professional_assessment.py`'s `ProfessionalAssessment.status` → `professional_assessment_status`; `risk_engine.py`'s `RiskAssessmentRun.status` → `risk_assessment_run_status`.
- `CustomerType`: `customer.py`'s `Customer.customer_type` → `master_customer_type`; `onboarding.py`'s `OnboardingSession.customer_type` → `onboarding_customer_type`.
- `NoteType`: `case.py`'s `CaseNote.note_type` → `case_note_type`; `customer.py`'s `CustomerNote.note_type` → `customer_note_type`.
- `RecommendationPriority`/`RecommendationStatus`: `independent_review.py`'s `ReviewRecommendation.priority`/`.status` → `review_recommendation_priority`/`review_recommendation_status`; `regulatory_recommendation.py`'s `RegulatoryRecommendation.priority`/`.status` → `regulatory_recommendation_priority`/`regulatory_recommendation_status`.
- `ReviewOutcome`: `customer.py`'s `CustomerReview.outcome` → `customer_review_outcome`; `professional_assessment.py`'s four columns sharing the enum (`SOFAssessment.review_outcome`, `SOWAssessment.review_outcome`, `TransactionPurposeAssessment.review_outcome`, `InvestmentLegitimacyAssessment.review_outcome_status`) → `professional_review_outcome`.
- `TrainingStatus`: `aml_solution.py`'s `TrainingRecord.status` → `legacy_training_status`; `governance_training.py`'s `GovernanceTrainingRecord.status` → `governance_training_status`.
- New migration `alembic/versions/0d8a1fc0b65b_split_p47_duplicate_enum_types.py`, one revision covering all 8 groups, same idempotent shape as P37's: for a database still carrying any of the old shared types (`alertstatus`, `assessmentstatus`, `customertype`, `notetype`, `recommendationpriority`, `recommendationstatus`, `reviewoutcome`, `trainingstatus`), splits it into the new explicitly-named types with a `USING <col>::text::<new type>` cast and drops the old one; on a database built fresh after this fix, each group's split is a correct no-op (the baseline migration's `create_all()` already produces the split types from the fixed models).
**A real bug caught by live verification, not just code review:** the migration's first draft used the wrong column name for `InvestmentLegitimacyAssessment`'s `ReviewOutcome` column (`review_outcome` instead of the actual `review_outcome_status` — `review_outcome` is a *different*, plain `Text` column on that same model). Running it against a real database failed loudly (`DROP TYPE reviewoutcome` blocked by a still-dependent column) rather than silently mis-migrating, and cross-checking every `(table, column)` pair in the migration against `information_schema.columns` before re-running caught it for certain. Alembic's transactional DDL meant the failed first attempt rolled back cleanly with zero partial state.
**Confirmed the `CustomerType` collision was live, not theoretical, with the real bug's own reproduction:** on a genuinely fresh Postgres 16 database built through the entire migration chain, `POST /api/v1/onboarding/sessions` with `customer_type=business` now returns 201 with `"customer_type": "business"` actually persisted — this call was the exact scenario the original finding predicted would 500 (`onboarding.py`'s `CustomerType.business` didn't exist in the shared type `customer.py`'s 7 values had won). Also verified against the real, already-existing `verigo_dev` database (which still had all 8 old shared types intact, since only `ControlStatus`/P37 had been split there so far): all 8 splits applied cleanly, all 17 new types carry exactly the right value sets, all affected tables' data (0 rows in every case in this database) preserved, and a full downgrade → re-upgrade round-trip restores and re-splits cleanly.
**What did NOT change:** the 4 duplicate-named enums confirmed harmless in the original P47 finding (`AlertSeverity`, `CryptoNetwork`, `IFTIDirection`, `RiskLevel` — identical values on both sides, so no collision-driven bug exists) were left as-is; giving them explicit names too would be a pure hygiene improvement with no functional bug to fix, not part of this batch.
**Verified:** 3 new tests (`tests/test_p47_duplicate_enum_collisions_smoke.py`) — every one of the 9 affected columns carries its expected explicit type name; no two of the (now 9, previously up to 3-way-colliding) columns still share a type name; `CustomerType`'s value sets are confirmed to overlap only on `"individual"` (documenting why the default onboarding path masked the bug while every non-default path hit it). Full backend suite: 605 passed. mypy (CI's exact `mypy app/ --ignore-missing-imports --no-strict-optional` invocation) and ruff (check + format) clean on all changed/added files. Live Postgres verification as described above — both the "existing broken database" and "genuinely fresh database" paths, including a real HTTP business-onboarding call, not just a migration dry-run.
**Detail:** `app/models/monitoring.py`; `app/models/screening.py`; `app/models/aml_solution.py`; `app/models/professional_assessment.py`; `app/models/risk_engine.py`; `app/models/customer.py`; `app/models/onboarding.py`; `app/models/case.py`; `app/models/independent_review.py`; `app/models/regulatory_recommendation.py`; `app/models/governance_training.py`; `alembic/versions/0d8a1fc0b65b_split_p47_duplicate_enum_types.py`; `tests/test_p47_duplicate_enum_collisions_smoke.py`.

## Resolved on your direction (2026-09-11, "dig in")

### `GET /connectors/` crashed on every call — found digging into P45's decorative-marketplace question
**Found:** 2026-09-11, while tracing whether the deprecated legacy "Connector Marketplace" (`app/api/routes/connectors.py`, `connector_credentials` table) was itself a second architecture-duplication problem alongside the Integration Hub. It isn't — it's a clean, intentional deprecation (`POST /` explicitly 410s with a message pointing at the Hub; the docstring says so outright) — but reading the still-live `GET /` handler for context found its body computed an unused, tautological local (`industry_id = current_user.org_id if role != admin else current_user.org_id` — always the same value either way) and then fell off the end without calling `get_credentials()` (imported at the top of the file, never used) or returning anything.
**Why this matters despite the system being deprecated:** deprecated only means "no new connections" — `connectors.py`'s own docstring says existing credentials are meant to keep working, and the frontend `/connectors` page (still linked from the footer and mobile nav on every page) calls this exact endpoint to list them. Every call returned `None`, which `response_model=List[ConnectorResponse]` rejected with a `ResponseValidationError` — reproduced live via the test client: every single request 500s, regardless of whether any credentials exist for the org.
**Resolved:** same day, per your direction ("dig in").
**What changed:** `list_connectors()` now calls `get_credentials(db, current_user.org_id, provider=provider, organisation_id=current_user.primary_organisation_id)` — the same helper `update_connector`/`delete_credential`/`test_credential` already use in this file — and returns its result, matching the pattern used everywhere else in the module.
**Verified:** 3 new tests (`tests/test_connectors_list_smoke.py`) — an org with no credentials gets `200 []` instead of the crash; a real stored `ConnectorCredential` row is returned correctly (provider, key_hint fields serialise as expected); the `?provider=` filter works. Full backend suite: 608 passed. mypy and ruff clean on both changed files.
**Detail:** `app/api/routes/connectors.py`; `tests/test_connectors_list_smoke.py`.

## Resolved on your direction (2026-09-11, "p45")

### P45 — Integration Hub stops fabricating success; identity/KYC confirmed not a bug
**Found:** 2026-09-11, digging into P45 (see the "dug into" entry above for the full investigation). **Resolved:** same day, per your direction ("p45"), choosing the safer interim move (stop the false claims) over the larger "build real connections for 48 providers" project, which stays open as its own decision.
**What changed:**
- `POST /{slug}/test` (`app/api/routes/integrations.py`) no longer reports `health_status=healthy` or "Connection validated" from `bool(credentials_encrypted)` alone. It now always reports `health_status=unknown` with an honest message ("Credentials saved. Live connection verification isn't built yet for any provider in this catalog -- this only confirms something was stored, not that it works.") when credentials exist, or the original "No credentials configured." when they don't. `consecutive_failures`/health-degrade tracking tied to the old fake pass/fail was removed along with it, since there's no real signal to drive it from yet.
- `POST /{slug}/oauth/authorize` and `POST /{slug}/oauth/callback` now refuse with `501` ("OAuth2 connection for '{slug}' isn't implemented yet. No provider in this catalog has a real OAuth2 app configured.") instead of building a fake `*.example` authorize URL and, on callback, fabricating token pairs and marking the integration connected. `/authorize` still validates the provider exists and is genuinely OAuth2-typed before refusing (404/409 preserved), and no longer creates an `OrgIntegration` row or persists an `oauth_state` for a connection that was never going to happen.
- `web/app/api-integrations/page.tsx`: the OAuth step of the "Connect" wizard no longer shows a working "Connect with {provider}" button that silently succeeds (the frontend's own old comment admitted it "mocked" the round trip with a synthetic code, bypassing the fake URL entirely) — it now shows "{provider} uses OAuth2. This isn't set up yet..." with only a Close button. The non-OAuth result panel no longer shows a green "✓ Connection validated" checkmark; it shows a neutral "ⓘ Credentials saved..." message matching the backend's honest text. Removed the now-dead `startOAuth()` function and its now-unused `secrets`/`encrypt_secret`/`decrypt_secret` backend imports.
**What did NOT change:** credential storage itself (`enable_integration`, encryption, audit log, credential rotation, expiry alerting) was already real and honest — an org's own API keys really are stored, encrypted, and audit-logged; only the false "we verified this actually works" claim is gone. No provider actually gained real connectivity testing or OAuth — the bigger build-vs-relabel decision from the original P45 finding is still open, tracked in the table above.
**Verified:** 6 new backend tests (`tests/test_integration_hub_no_fake_success.py`) — `/test` reports `unknown` with the new message even with real credentials saved; the "no credentials" branch still reports correctly; `/oauth/authorize` refuses with 501 for a real OAuth2 provider (Salesforce) while still 404ing an unknown slug and 409ing a real non-OAuth2 provider; no `OrgIntegration` row is persisted by a refused authorize attempt; `/oauth/callback` refuses with 501 and cannot be used to fabricate a connection. Full backend suite: 614 passed. mypy and ruff clean (pre-existing `B008`/`UP045` warnings elsewhere in the file, unrelated to this change, left as-is per prior sessions' convention). Frontend: `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean (one pre-existing, unrelated `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` warning). **Verified live in a real browser**, not just statically: started the actual backend (against a real Postgres database) and the actual Next.js dev server, logged in as a real registered user through the real login form, opened the Integration Hub, confirmed the OAuth wizard (HubSpot) shows the new "isn't set up yet" copy with no working Connect action, and confirmed the API-key wizard (FrankieOne) shows the neutral "Credentials saved..." message with no checkmark or "validated" claim anywhere on the page after a real save-and-test round trip.
**Detail:** `app/api/routes/integrations.py`; `web/app/api-integrations/page.tsx`; `tests/test_integration_hub_no_fake_success.py`.

## Resolved on your direction (2026-09-11, "p45 build the real one")

### Integration Hub `/test` now makes a genuinely real vendor call for 7 providers
**Requested:** 2026-09-11, after you picked the scoped option offered ("only the providers with a real adapter already") over building all 48 from scratch, and confirmed OAuth2 providers stay out of scope for now (no specific provider named, credential handoff would be via env vars once one is).
**What changed:**
- New `app/services/integration_verify.py` — one real, read-only check per provider, reusing the exact adapter classes already used (and already tested) elsewhere in this codebase: `SumsubProvider.generate_access_token()` (Sumsub), `ComplyAdvantageProvider.screen()` (a benign test name), `ChainalysisProvider`/`EllipticProvider.screen_address()` (against the well-known Bitcoin genesis address — safe, real, harmless), `ABNLookupProvider.search_by_name()` (ABR). Two new, small checks for providers with no existing adapter but a standard, documented verification call: Twilio's account-fetch endpoint, and a shared SMTP EHLO+STARTTLS+LOGIN handshake (no message sent) for SendGrid/SES, which are both used via SMTP relay in this app already (see the SendGrid factory fix below).
- `POST /{slug}/test` (`app/api/routes/integrations.py`) now decrypts the org's real stored credentials and calls this dispatcher for the 7 wired slugs, reporting a genuinely earned `health_status=healthy`/`down` from the real result. Every other provider (no adapter, or an unregistered OAuth2 provider) keeps the honest `unknown`/"not built yet" response from the prior P45 fix — never fabricated.
- **`required_credentials` populated for the first time for these 7 providers** — a real, previously-undiscovered gap found while wiring this: `IntegrationProvider.required_credentials` was never set by `_seed_providers()` for *any* of the 48 providers, so the credential form always fell back to one generic "API Key" field for everyone, wrong for Sumsub (needs `app_token`+`secret_key`), Elliptic (`api_key`+`api_secret`), Twilio (`account_sid`+`auth_token`), ABR (`guid`), and SendGrid/SES (3-field SMTP relay credentials) alike. Fixed by adding real `required_credentials` schemas to those 7 `PROVIDER_CATALOG` entries and changing `_seed_providers()` to backfill the field on already-seeded rows (it previously only ever inserted missing rows, the same staleness shape as P37/P47's enum-seeding gaps) — verified live against the real `verigo_dev` database, which had all 8 stale empty arrays before the backfill and the correct real schemas after.
- **Fixed a second real, previously-undiscovered bug found in the same area:** `app/integrations/email/factory.py` imported `.sendgrid` (`SendGridEmailProvider`) for `EMAIL_PROVIDER=sendgrid`, but `app/integrations/email/sendgrid.py` doesn't exist — this branch would `ModuleNotFoundError` the moment anyone set that env var (currently latent since the default is `"smtp"`, but a real landmine for a future misconfiguration). Removed the dead branch; SendGrid works today via the existing `SMTPEmailProvider` and SendGrid's own documented SMTP relay (`smtp.sendgrid.net`, username `apikey`), per `smtp.py`'s own docstring ("Works with any SMTP relay (SendGrid, Postmark, SES, etc.)").
**What did NOT change / stays honestly scoped:** these checks test only whether *these specific org-entered credentials* work against the real vendor — they never touch or reconfigure VeriGo's own platform-wide screening/KYC/email pipelines (`app.config.settings`), which is a deliberately separate, untouched configuration surface. The other 41 providers (no real adapter exists, or an OAuth2 provider with no registered app) are unchanged — still `unknown`/501, honestly. A pre-existing gap found but not fixed in `abn_lookup.py`: `search_by_name()` never raises `ProviderRejectedError` for an invalid GUID (ABR returns HTTP 200 with an embedded `<exception>` element, which this method doesn't parse — unlike `lookup_abn`/`lookup_acn`, which do, via `_parse_entity`, though even those silently swallow it back into a `None` return rather than propagating it) — so the ABR connection check can only prove reachability, not that the GUID itself is genuinely valid; documented in code and left as a separate, small, pre-existing item, not part of this task.
**None of these 7 real checks were run against a real vendor account.** No live credentials for any of them existed in this session, and this sandbox's egress is blocked to most of these domains anyway — confirmed live: submitting a fake Sumsub App Token/Secret Key through the real browser UI produced a real "✗ Could not reach Sumsub: 403 Forbidden" (the sandbox's own egress proxy blocking the attempt), not a fabricated success or the old placeholder text — proof the code makes a genuine outbound call rather than faking a result, but not proof any of the seven integrations work against a real account. Confirm each against a real vendor account before relying on it in production.
**Verified:** 20 new tests across two files — `tests/test_integration_verify_real_checks.py` (18 tests: success/failure/missing-field paths for all 7 providers, mocking `httpx.AsyncClient`/`smtplib.SMTP` the same way `test_crypto_wallet_screening.py` already tests `OFACSDNProvider`) and 3 new tests added to `tests/test_integration_hub_no_fake_success.py` (route-level: an unwired provider still reports `unknown`; Sumsub now genuinely reports `healthy` or `down` depending on the mocked vendor response; the credential form now asks for the real `app_token`/`secret_key` fields instead of the generic fallback). Full backend suite: 635 passed. mypy (CI's exact invocation) and ruff (check + format) clean on all changed/added files (pre-existing `B008` in `integrations.py`, unrelated, left as-is). **Verified live in a real browser** against the real `verigo_dev` Postgres database: the Sumsub connect wizard now renders the real 2-field form (confirmed via screenshot), and a real save-and-test round trip through the actual backend produced the real, honest `403 Forbidden` result described above rather than any fabricated message.
**Detail:** `app/services/integration_verify.py`; `app/api/routes/integrations.py`; `app/models/integration.py`; `app/integrations/email/factory.py`; `tests/test_integration_verify_real_checks.py`; `tests/test_integration_hub_no_fake_success.py`.

### P23 — Accountants now has a content-rewritten AML/risk template matching the real VERIGO_ACCOUNTANT_* document suite
**Parked:** 2026-09-08, as real content-authoring work needing sign-off — the last of the 8-sector comparison. **Resolved:** 2026-09-11, per your direction selecting "Industry template rewrites" as the priority, closing out the batch as the fifth and final sector after Real Estate (P22).

The **Tax Practitioners Board / Tax Agent Services Act 2009 dual-regulatory regime** — tax structuring advice that crosses from legitimate minimisation into concealment of criminal income is both a TPB Code breach and a potential SMR trigger, with a parallel ATO referral consideration — is now a first-class, recurring theme (previously entirely absent). `independent_review` fixed to annual, given the matrix's four CRITICAL-rated risks. 6 new controls and 4 new policies added covering entity formation, nominee services, managed client funds, and the tax-structuring/TPB interface.

### P24 — Cross-cutting operational-log data model: two of four "missing" concepts already existed; the one genuine gap (SMR Internal Decision Log) is built, and ECDD case tracking is generalised to all 8 industries
**Parked:** 2026-09-08/09, as a product-wide structural gap needing its own design decision rather than a side effect of any one sector's content fix. **Resolved:** 2026-09-11/12, per your direction to proceed after choosing "dedicated tables per log type" for the genuine gap, then "generalise ProfessionalAssessment to all industries" once investigation found the premise behind that first choice was only half right.
**What changed:** Before building anything, checked whether the 4 operational-log concepts flagged as "no model support anywhere" actually had no support — 2 of the 4 already did:
- **TMP Alert Log** → already fully implemented as `TransactionAlert` (`app/models/monitoring.py`) — rule-triggered or manual (`alert_type=manual`), full review/outcome/SMR-candidate workflow. No new table built; would have been a duplicate/competing system (exactly what PARKING_LOT's own section A warns about).
- **Sanctions Screening Log** → already implemented as the unified `ScreeningRecord` (`app/models/screening.py`). No new table built, same reasoning.
- **ECDD Case File** → genuinely incomplete: `ProfessionalAssessment` (`app/models/professional_assessment.py`) already provided the full shape (SOF/SOW/transaction-purpose sub-assessments, a configurable checklist), but its `ProfessionalServiceType` enum only covered 6 "professional services" industries — Remittance, VASP and DPMS had no structured ECDD case file at all. Per your direction, generalised rather than forked: added `remittance`, `vasp`, `dpms` to `ProfessionalServiceType` (kept the class/module name as-is — documented in the module docstring as legacy naming that now covers all 8 sectors, to avoid an unforced rename across the API/service layer for no functional gain), and added `DEFAULT_CHECKLISTS` entries for all 3 new industries, each grounded in real content already written this session (Remittance: third-party sender CDD, corridor risk register; VASP: blockchain analytics/Travel Rule/VASP-to-VASP counterparty DD; DPMS: the $10k cash threshold, provenance/sanctioned-origin, buy-back pattern, Director sign-off — sourced from `app/templates/aml/industries/{remittance,vasp,dpms}.py`, not invented). Also gave the `professional_service_type` column an explicit `name="professionalservicetype"` (hardens against the P37/P47 enum-collision bug class going forward, at zero functional change — it matches the type name SQLAlchemy already derived by default).
- **SMR Internal Decision Log** → the one real gap. `Case` already has `is_smr_candidate`/`smr_considered`/`smr_lodged` boolean fields, but every real Program requires a retained decision record for **every** escalated suspicion, including one cleared without ever becoming a Case and a documented decision *not* to lodge — Case's fields can't represent that. Built new: `app/models/smr_decision_log.py` (`SMRDecisionLog` + 4 enums), linking optionally to `Customer`, `Case`, `TransactionAlert` (the originating TMP alert, if any) and `ProfessionalAssessment` (the originating ECDD case, if any) so a decision's provenance is traceable without forcing every suspicion through a full Case. Fields mirror the real Accountants SMR Guideline's Appendix A (Decision Log) and Appendix B (Pre-Lodgement Checklist), re-read in full this session for exact field fidelity — matter source, suspicion category, risk-matrix ref, CO assessment, s.41 suspicion-formed flag (human decision only, never auto-set), the 24-hour (terrorism financing) / 3-business-day (all other matters) SMR deadline, tipping-off confirmation, AUSTRAC reference, director notification, and post-decision review date.
- `app/api/routes/smr_decision_log.py` — new minimal CRUD API (`create` / `list` / `get` / `assess` / `lodge` / `close`), mirroring `professional_assessment.py`'s existing route conventions (org-scoped queries, `Pagination`, `require_analyst_or_above`/`require_compliance_or_above`, audit logging via `audit_service.log_action`). `lodge` refuses to proceed without `tipping_off_check_confirmed=true` and without `suspicion_formed=true` on the same record, matching the real Guideline's mandatory pre-lodgement gate. Wired into `app/main.py`.
- `alembic/versions/a3f5c8d1e9b2_p24_ecdd_scope_and_smr_decision_log.py` — new migration: adds the 3 enum values to `professionalservicetype`, creates `smr_decision_logs` and its 4 enum types. Guards the table/type creation on an inspector check (`if "smr_decision_logs" in inspector.get_table_names(): return`) since a genuinely fresh database built via this repo's `baseline_sync_with_models` migration (`Base.metadata.create_all()`) already creates the table from the current model state — verified both paths live (see below).
**Bug found and fixed along the way:** verifying the new migration against a truly fresh database (rather than an upgraded one, which is what the original fix was tested against) reproduced a real, separate bug in the earlier IFTI migration fix: `alembic/versions/f1a2b3c4d5e6_ifti_dra_maker_checker_workflow.py`'s `UPDATE ifti_records SET status = 'under_review' WHERE status = 'ready'` fails on any database where the `iftistatus` enum type never had `'ready'` as a member to begin with — which is every fresh install, since `create_all()` builds the type from the current model (already `'ready'`-free). Postgres validates an enum literal at parse time, so the bare comparison raised `InvalidTextRepresentation` before a single row was scanned, breaking `alembic upgrade head` on a clean database outright. Fixed by casting to text (`status::text = 'ready'`), which matches rows on an old, upgraded database and is a safe no-op everywhere else.
**Verified:** stood up a real local Postgres 16 instance and ran the full migration chain from empty (`alembic upgrade head`) — confirmed it now completes cleanly end to end (previously broken by the bug above), and confirmed the P24 migration itself via both paths: fresh-database (table pre-created by baseline `create_all()`, migration's inspector guard correctly no-ops) and upgraded-database (downgraded to the prior revision, re-ran the migration, confirmed it takes the explicit `create_table` path and produces an identical schema). Round-tripped both new capabilities against the real database directly through the SQLAlchemy models: inserted and re-fetched an `SMRDecisionLog` row, and a `ProfessionalAssessment` row using the new `remittance` enum value. Added `tests/test_p24_smr_decision_log_smoke.py` (4 tests: create→assess→lodge, lodge rejected without tipping-off confirmation, clear-without-SMR, org-scoped tenant isolation) — all pass. Full existing suite re-run after all changes: 639 passed (up from the prior 122-test targeted subset, since this now touches shared model/route/migration infrastructure). Ruff clean on all changed/new files; mypy shows the same pre-existing errors already present in `governance.py`/the two `factory.py` files, plus the same `Column(String) -> str | None` typing gap already present in the shipped `professional_assessment.py` route (tracked separately as P5, not introduced by this change).
**Detail:** `app/models/smr_decision_log.py` (new); `app/models/professional_assessment.py`; `app/models/__init__.py`; `app/api/routes/smr_decision_log.py` (new); `app/main.py`; `alembic/versions/a3f5c8d1e9b2_p24_ecdd_scope_and_smr_decision_log.py` (new); `alembic/versions/f1a2b3c4d5e6_ifti_dra_maker_checker_workflow.py` (bug fix); `tests/test_p24_smr_decision_log_smoke.py` (new).
