## C2 pass 9, 2026-09-16 (oversized route files — fourth of 5: `screening.py`)

**Scope:** fourth of the 5 oversized route files, `screening.py` (1570 lines, 28 top-level functions / 18 endpoints, the Screening Hub — sanctions/PEP/watchlist/adverse-media/crypto-wallet screening). Same discipline as passes 6-8: read the whole file first, split along real domain boundaries, verify every function's exact source and every endpoint's permission dependency programmatically before committing.

**What changed:** split eight ways:
- **`quick_screen.py`** — ad-hoc, not-yet-customer-linked lookup (1 endpoint).
- **`records.py`** — the screening-record lifecycle: run/list/get/re-screen (4 endpoints), plus its own `_severity_for_type` helper.
- **`dashboard.py`** — org-wide health summary aggregating across records, alerts, crypto wallets and adverse media (1 endpoint) — kept separate from records.py since it doesn't belong to any single domain.
- **`alerts.py`** — alert listing and management: list/review/assign (3 endpoints) — `list_alerts` moved here from the original's "Screening Records" section since it's genuinely alert-domain, not record-domain.
- **`batch.py`** — multi-customer batch screening (1 endpoint).
- **`crypto_wallet.py`** — crypto wallet screening: screen/list (2 endpoints).
- **`adverse_media.py`** — adverse media findings: record/list/review (3 endpoints).
- **`customer_summary.py`** — per-customer screening summary and the identity-verification score/decision that gates KYC status (3 endpoints).

`_log`, `_record_dict`, `_alert_dict`, `_resolve_customer` and the sanctions/PEP screening chain (`_screen`, `_run_real_screening`, `_simulate_screening`, `_sanctions_result_to_dict`, `_pep_result_to_dict`, `DISCLAIMER`, `_SANCTIONS_TYPES`, `_PEP_TYPES`) — all genuinely used by 2+ domain files — moved to `_shared.py`; `_severity_for_type` (used only by `run_screening`) and `_QUICK_SCREEN_CATEGORIES` (used only by `quick_screen`) stayed domain-local.

**A FastAPI gotcha found while wiring `__init__.py`, not present in the previous three splits:** `records.py`'s `list_screening_records` is `GET ""` (the bare `/screening` path). FastAPI's `include_router()` refuses an empty combined prefix + empty route path outright — it has no visibility into the fact that the *parent* router's own prefix will fill the gap — so a straightforward `router = APIRouter(prefix="/screening", ...); router.include_router(records.router)` (the pattern used in every earlier split) raised `FastAPIError: Prefix and path cannot be both empty`. Fixed by giving the package's own `router` no prefix and instead passing `prefix="/screening"` explicitly to every `include_router()` call in `__init__.py` — resolves to byte-identical final paths (confirmed below), just sidesteps the check. Also confirmed (empirically, against the already-merged `risk_assessment`/`governance-training`/`reports` packages) that OpenAPI tags survive this restructuring either way — `include_router()` merges the *including* router's own `.tags` into every route it adds, independent of prefix, so `tags=["Screening Hub"]` on the outer router still applies correctly.

**Cross-file route-ordering constraint, same shape as `/smr/enums` and the `reports.py` filing-register fix, but spanning 3 files instead of 1:** `records.py`'s `GET /{record_id}` is a single-segment path-parameter route that would shadow any literal single-segment `GET` route registered after it. `GET /dashboard` (`dashboard.py`) and `GET /alerts` (`alerts.py`) are exactly such routes, so `__init__.py` must `include_router()` them *before* `records.py` — documented in `__init__.py`'s own docstring and verified live (see below), not just by the AST/openapi diffs which can't see registration order across files.

**Verification, same method as passes 6-8:** a script comparing every function's exact AST source segment, original file vs. all 9 new files — 28 of 28 original top-level functions present, 0 missing, 0 extra, 0 body mismatches. A second check confirmed every endpoint's `current_user: User = Depends(...)` dependency name is unchanged. A full `app.openapi()` diff — path, method, operationId *and tags* — is byte-for-byte identical between the original file and the new package (691 operations). This pass's manual-retyping mistakes were caught by these same checks before they ever reached a commit: a `list_screening_records` query-param that lost its `Optional[...]` type hints, and two missing imports (`uuid4` in `adverse_media.py`, `AdverseMediaResult` in `customer_summary.py`) — the latter two caught by `ruff`/import-check, the type-hint one caught by re-reading the diff against the original before running any check at all.

**Verified:** all new files parse; `app.main` imports cleanly (697 routes); `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` (CI's exact flags, after `ruff --fix` sorted two import blocks) and `mypy` (CI's exact flags) both clean; full suite 905 passed, 2 skipped, 0 regressions, on both default SQLite and a real local Postgres 16 instance — including the dedicated crypto-wallet/sanctions-PEP/audit/pagination/webhook-dispatch tests (60/60, after updating 5 `monkeypatch.setattr("app.api.routes.screening.X", ...)` targets in 3 test files to their new submodule locations — `get_crypto_provider` now lives in `crypto_wallet.py`, `get_pep_provider` in `_shared.py`, `dispatch_event_background` in `records.py` — since a monkeypatch has to target where a name is actually imported and used, not a re-export) — plus a live throwaway test confirming `/screening/dashboard`, `/screening/alerts`, `/screening` (list) and `/screening/{record_id}` (404 case) all resolve to their correct handlers, not each other.
**Detail:** `app/api/routes/screening.py` deleted; new `app/api/routes/screening/{__init__,_shared,quick_screen,records,dashboard,alerts,batch,crypto_wallet,adverse_media,customer_summary}.py`; `tests/test_crypto_wallet_screening.py`, `tests/test_sanctions_pep_screening_real_provider.py`, `tests/test_webhook_dispatch_on_real_events.py` updated for the new monkeypatch targets.

**Remaining:** `customers.py` (2377 lines) — the last and largest of the 5, deliberately saved for last.

## C2 pass 10, 2026-09-16 (oversized route files — fifth and final of 5: `customers.py`)

**Scope:** the last and largest of the 5 oversized route files, `customers.py` (2377 lines, 53 top-level functions / ~46 endpoints, the Customer & KYC/KYB API), deliberately saved for last. Same discipline as passes 6-9: read the whole file first, split along real domain boundaries, verify every function's exact source and every endpoint's permission dependency programmatically before committing.

**What changed:** split ten ways:
- **`crud.py`** — core Customer CRUD: create/list/get/update/status-change (5 endpoints), plus its own `_SCREENING_BLOCKING_STATUSES`.
- **`kyc_identity.py`** — individual KYC evidence: previous names, identity documents, selfie verification, address verification (8 endpoints) — these four sections aren't contiguous in the original file (Business Detail/Beneficial Owners/Corporate Documents sit between Previous Names and Identity Documents), regrouped here by actual domain rather than original position.
- **`kyb_business.py`** — KYB business evidence: business detail, beneficial owners, corporate documents (7 endpoints).
- **`contact_verification.py`** — phone and email OTP verification (4 endpoints).
- **`customer_screening.py`** — per-customer screening: trigger/list/review, crypto wallet screening, screening alerts (7 endpoints), plus its own `_SCREENING_CHECKLIST_FLAG` — named to avoid confusion with the unrelated standalone Screening Hub package at `app.api.routes.screening` (a different, fully-qualified module; this one is the customer-scoped `/customers/{id}/...` subset).
- **`compliance_status.py`** — onboarding checklist, risk score history and rescoring, pre-approval question checklist (5 endpoints).
- **`reviews_notes.py`** — periodic reviews, compliance notes (4 endpoints).
- **`workspace.py`** — the aggregated read-only workspace view and synthesized timeline (2 endpoints, large: ~300 lines combined), plus its own `_customer_checklist_summary` and `DISCLAIMER` (the only two of the original's module-level names used by exactly one domain).
- **`override.py`** — the MLRO unified override (1 endpoint).
- **`bulk_import.py`** — CSV/Excel bulk import: template download, field guide, upload (3 endpoints).

`_get_customer`, `_next_customer_ref`, `_create_checklist`, `_update_checklist_flag` (+its private `_check_checklist_complete`), and `_record_risk_history` — all genuinely used by 2+ domain files (`_get_customer` by literally every endpoint) — moved to `_shared.py`, along with the shared `log = logging.getLogger(...)`. Two originally-unused imports in the source file (`CustomerQuestionAnswerItem`, `compute_final_approval_score` — dead since before this split, confirmed via grep, nothing in the file body ever referenced them) were dropped rather than carried into any new file, since redistributing imports by actual usage is what this kind of split does anyway.

**Route-ordering analysis, done but this time finding no hazard:** unlike the `reports.py` and `screening.py` splits, `customers.py` turned out to have no route-registration-order risk at all. The only path-parameter route at the top level, `GET /{customer_id}` (in `crud.py`), is a single path segment; every other route in the package is at least two segments (`/{customer_id}/<literal>`) or a two-segment literal (`/import/<literal>`), so none of them can be shadowed by `/{customer_id}` regardless of which order `__init__.py` includes the sub-routers in. Checked deliberately (not assumed) by walking every route's segment count before writing `__init__.py`, since assuming "no hazard" without checking is exactly how the `reports.py` filing-register bug and the screening.py dashboard/alerts ordering went unnoticed for as long as they did in the original code.

**The same FastAPI empty-path gotcha as the `screening.py` split, for the same reason:** `crud.py`'s `list_customers`/`create_customer` are `GET ""`/`POST ""` (the bare `/customers` path), so `__init__.py` again gives the package's own `router` no prefix and passes `prefix="/customers"` explicitly on every `include_router()` call instead — same fix, same reasoning as documented in `screening/__init__.py`.

**Verification, same method as passes 6-9:** a script comparing every function's exact AST source segment, original file vs. all 11 new files — 53 of 53 original top-level functions present, 0 missing, 0 extra, 0 body mismatches. A second check confirmed every endpoint's `current_user: User = Depends(...)` dependency name is unchanged. A full `app.openapi()` diff — path, method, operationId *and tags* — is byte-for-byte identical between the original file and the new package (691 operations, same as before this split since it's a pure move). Two import-block sort issues (`ruff --fix`) were the only findings this pass — a lower defect count than any of the previous three splits, consistent with this file having a simpler route-ordering shape to get wrong.

**Verified:** all new files parse; `app.main` imports cleanly (697 routes); `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` (CI's exact flags) and `mypy` (CI's exact flags) both clean; full suite 905 passed, 2 skipped, 0 regressions, on both default SQLite and a real local Postgres 16 instance — including the dedicated customer/KYC/bulk-import/webhook tests (38/38, after updating 2 `monkeypatch.setattr("app.api.routes.customers.dispatch_event_background", ...)` targets in `tests/test_webhook_dispatch_on_real_events.py` to `app.api.routes.customers.crud.dispatch_event_background`, the submodule where that name now actually lives and is used — same category of fix as the `screening.py` split's monkeypatch retargeting).
**Detail:** `app/api/routes/customers.py` deleted; new `app/api/routes/customers/{__init__,_shared,crud,kyc_identity,kyb_business,contact_verification,customer_screening,compliance_status,reviews_notes,workspace,override,bulk_import}.py`; `tests/test_webhook_dispatch_on_real_events.py` updated for the new monkeypatch target.

**C2's oversized-route-files sub-item is now fully resolved — 5 of 5 files done** (`risk_assessment.py`, `governance/training.py`, `reports.py`, `screening.py`, `customers.py`). C2 overall is 4 of 6 sub-items done. Remaining: no central frontend API client (~35+ pages), thin `web/components/ui/` (only `button.tsx`/`card.tsx`).

## C2 pass 11, 2026-09-16 (frontend API client — scaffold + one pilot resource)

**Scope:** the first of C2's two remaining sub-items, narrowed deliberately rather than attempted as a single 44-file migration (per your direction to survey first, then scope down). Surveyed the frontend before writing anything: `lib/auth.ts` already exports `apiFetch()` (Bearer token + CSRF header injection), used at 214 call sites across 44 files — a partial client already existed, so this wasn't a blank-slate build. The actual gaps: all 44 files independently re-declare the same `const API = process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL ?? "..."` line; every call site hand-builds its own URL string and its own inline `res.ok`/`res.json()`/try-catch (zero TypeScript response interfaces anywhere — everything implicitly `any`); 30 call sites silently swallow fetch errors in an empty `catch {}`; no axios/SWR/react-query, just raw `fetch`. Picked `customers` as the pilot resource since its backend route package was just finished (C2 pass 10) and its exact real-world call sites (5 files, 7 call sites) were easy to enumerate precisely.

**What changed:**
- **`web/lib/api/client.ts`** (new) — the shared layer every call site was reinventing: one `API_BASE` constant, and `apiGet`/`apiPost`/`apiPatch`/`apiDelete` built on the existing `apiFetch`, all going through one `parseJsonOrThrow()` that throws a typed `ApiError` (message from the response's `.detail`, matching the parse-or-throw convention `lib/signup.ts` already had privately as its own local `asJson()` — this generalises that same pattern instead of inventing a new one).
- **`web/lib/api/customers.ts`** (new) — typed functions for exactly the customer endpoints the frontend actually calls today (`listCustomers`, `getCustomerWorkspace`, `getCustomerTimeline`, `overrideCustomer`, `createCustomer`), not the full ~46-endpoint backend surface. Types (`CustomerSummary`, `CustomerOverrideInput`, `CustomerCreateInput`) mirror `app/schemas/customer.py`'s `CustomerResponse`/`CustomerOverrideRequest`/`CustomerCreate` field-for-field, confirmed against the real running backend (see Verified below), not guessed from the frontend's own assumptions.
- Migrated the 5 real call-site files (`app/customers/page.tsx`, `app/customers/[id]/page.tsx`, `app/ecdd/page.tsx`, `app/monitoring/page.tsx`, `lib/signup.ts`) onto the new client, removing their local `API` constant re-declarations and inline fetch/parse boilerplate for the customer calls specifically (each file's *other* resource calls — reports, alerts, transactions — were left untouched, out of this pilot's scope).

**A live, reachable bug found and fixed as a direct consequence of typing the response instead of leaving it `any`:** `app/customers/page.tsx` assigned the raw `/customers` list response straight into state typed against `lib/demoCustomers.ts`'s demo `Customer` shape (`customer_id`, numeric `id`), but the real backend returns `CustomerResponse`'s shape (`id`, `customer_ref` — no `customer_id` field at all). The page's filter, CSV export, and — critically — its `href={`/customers/${c.customer_id}`}` navigation link all read `c.customer_id`, so the instant a real API call succeeded, `.toLowerCase()` on `undefined` inside `.filter()` would throw during render and crash the whole page. Only demo data (which does carry `customer_id`) ever exercised this path safely before. Fixed by mapping `CustomerSummary[]` into the page's existing `Customer[]` shape explicitly (`toPageCustomer()`) instead of trusting the raw response — the render code and its type contract are otherwise unchanged. A second, latent (not live) version of the same class of mismatch was in `lib/signup.ts`'s `createFirstCustomer()`, which declared a return type of `{ customer_id, full_name }` that the real endpoint never actually returns (its one caller never read the result, so this was dormant) — fixed the same way, mapping `id -> customer_id` explicitly rather than leaving the lie in the type signature.

**A smaller correctness fix along the way:** `app/ecdd/page.tsx`'s customer-search picker defensively handled two possible response shapes (`Array.isArray(d) ? d : d.items || []`), guarding against a paginated-wrapper shape the real `list_customers` endpoint (`response_model=List[CustomerResponse]`) never actually returns. Confirmed live against the backend and simplified to just the array shape.

**Verified:** every claim above about the real response shape was checked against a live local backend (registered a test user, created a real customer, then called list/search/create/workspace/timeline/override over HTTP) rather than assumed from the Pydantic schema alone — response bodies matched the new TypeScript interfaces field-for-field. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run lint` clean (0 errors; the 21 pre-existing warnings are all in files this pass didn't touch); `npm test` (vitest) 19/19 passed; `npm run build` (production Next.js build, which runs its own TypeScript pass) succeeded and generated all 113 routes including `/customers` and `/customers/[id]`. A browser-based end-to-end smoke test (Playwright, logged in against the live backend) was attempted but abandoned as unreliable in this sandboxed environment: it non-deterministically reported either a CORS-wildcard failure or the app's own PWA service-worker "offline" fallback intercepting the exact same request across otherwise-identical runs, while direct `curl` against the same backend and port never failed once — a sandbox networking artifact (proxying/interception between the browser and backend processes), not a defect in the client or the migrated pages, but noted here since it means this pass's confidence rests on schema/HTTP-level verification plus a clean build rather than a literal pixels-on-screen check.
**Detail:** new `web/lib/api/{client,customers}.ts`; `web/app/customers/page.tsx`, `web/app/customers/[id]/page.tsx`, `web/app/ecdd/page.tsx`, `web/app/monitoring/page.tsx`, `web/lib/signup.ts` updated.

**Remaining:** the other 39 files' ~207 call sites (billing, reports, screening, alerts, transactions, governance, etc.) still need the same per-resource-file treatment, one resource at a time as `customers` established the pattern; the 30 silent `catch {}` error-swallowing sites are a related but separate cleanup, not addressed by this pass beyond the ones inside the 5 migrated files. Thin `web/components/ui/` remains untouched.

## C2 pass 12, 2026-09-16 (frontend API client — second pilot resource: `analytics`)

**Scope:** the second resource migrated onto the `lib/api/` pattern established in C2 pass 11, picked after surveying the remaining 39 files by call-site/file count. `analytics` was the clear next candidate: 4 files (`app/analytics/{executive,compliance,mlro,operations}/page.tsx`), 19 call sites, all GET-only dashboard reads against one clean backend resource (`app/api/routes/analytics.py`) — unlike `governance` (higher call count but actually 3 separate backend resources sharing a URL prefix, deferred for its own pass) or `onboarding` (mixes the authenticated wizard with public token-based pages, a different auth model).

**What changed:**
- **`web/lib/api/analytics.ts`** (new) — typed functions for the 11 distinct analytics endpoints the frontend actually calls (out of `analytics.py`'s 13 total — `customers/risk-breakdown` and `audit/activity-trend` are never called from the frontend and were left out, same "type what's used" discipline as `customers.ts`). None of these routes declare a `response_model`; the actual return shape lives in `app/services/analytics_service.py`'s dict-building functions, so types were written by reading those directly, not guessed from the route file.
- Migrated all 4 pages onto it, removing their local `API` constant and inline fetch/parse boilerplate.

**A real design decision the migration forced, caught before it caused a bug:** every `by_status`/`by_risk`/`by_type`/`by_severity` field in these responses is a dict built by iterating a Python enum (`for level in RiskLevel: breakdown[level.value] = ...`), so its keys aren't fixed at the type level — they're whatever that enum currently has. Each of the 4 pages' demo-data fallback objects, however, hardcode only a handful of specific keys (e.g. `{low, medium, high, critical}`), which TypeScript infers as an exact literal shape. Typing these fields as `Record<string, number>` (the honest reflection of how the backend actually builds them) doesn't structurally satisfy state types inferred from those narrower demo literals, so `tsc` correctly refused to compile the migration as first written. Fixed by casting the demo objects' `by_*` fields to `Record<string, number>` at their declaration (not by inventing a false fixed-key type) — confirmed necessary and correct against the live backend: real responses for training status, case status and report status all carry more distinct keys than any of the 4 pages' demo data assumed (e.g. training has an `expired` status, cases have `additional_information`, reports have `under_review`/`approved` — none present in the hardcoded demo objects). A fixed-literal type would have been actively wrong here, not just less convenient.

**A smaller, related fix:** `app/analytics/compliance/page.tsx`'s demo data generated onboarding-trend and report-submission-trend series with a `volume` field via a shared `makeTrend()` helper, but the real endpoints backing those two (`customer_onboarding_trend`, `report_submission_trend`) only ever return `{date, count}` — no volume, confirmed against `analytics_service.py` and the live backend both. The field was already unused in this page's rendering (both series render with `valueKey="count"` only), so this was inert rather than a live bug like the `customers.py` pilot's, but left as-is it would have made the demo/live data shapes silently diverge; stripped it from the two demo series to match reality.

**Verified:** every claim about real response shapes was checked against the same live local backend used for the `customers` pilot (registered a fresh user, called all 11 endpoints over HTTP) — response bodies matched the new TypeScript interfaces exactly, and the `Record<string, number>` decision above was confirmed necessary rather than assumed. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, same 21 pre-existing warnings, none in touched files); `npm test` 19/19; `npm run build` succeeded, all 113 routes generated including the 4 analytics pages.
**Detail:** new `web/lib/api/analytics.ts`; `web/app/analytics/{executive,compliance,mlro,operations}/page.tsx` updated.

**Remaining:** 35 files/~188 call sites (governance's 3 sub-resources, billing, onboarding, organisations, reports, screening, alerts, transactions, etc.).

## C2 pass 13, 2026-09-16 (frontend API client — third pilot resource: `billing`)

**Scope:** the third resource migrated onto the `lib/api/` pattern from C2 pass 11. Turned out bigger than the original survey estimated: `app/billing/page.tsx` calls two genuinely separate backend resources under one file (`/api/v1/billing/*`, 12 call sites, and `/api/v1/storage/*`, 5 call sites — a different route file, `app/api/routes/storage.py`, for file-storage-vault quota config). Only the `billing/*` calls were migrated; the `storage/*` calls were deliberately left on `apiFetch`/`API` as-is, same discipline as leaving `ecdd.py`'s non-customer calls alone in the `customers` pilot.

**A deliberate exclusion, not an oversight:** `lib/pricing.ts`'s `fetchPlanPrices()` also calls `GET /billing/plans`, but wasn't migrated. It's a public, unauthenticated, server-side/ISR-cached fetch (`next: { revalidate: 300 }`) — a different execution context from `apiFetch()`'s client-only (`'use client'`) Bearer-token/CSRF handling, which reads `localStorage`/`document.cookie`. Routing a public marketing-page fetch through the client-auth wrapper would add machinery it doesn't need for zero benefit, so it keeps its own local `API` constant and raw `fetch()`.

**What changed:**
- **`web/lib/api/billing.ts`** (new) — typed functions for all 13 of `billing.py`'s customer-facing and (super-)admin endpoints the frontend actually calls (`listPlans`, `getMySubscription`, `listInvoices`, `createCheckout`, `changePlan`, `getCustomerPortalUrl`, `cancelSubscription`, `adminListAllSubscriptions`, `adminUpdateSubscription`, `adminActivateSubscription`, `adminTerminateSubscription`, `adminFeatureMatrix`, `adminToggleFeature`). Types mirror `app/schemas/billing.py` exactly (`SubscriptionResponse`, `InvoiceResponse`, `FeatureToggleRow`, `CheckoutSessionResponse`, `CustomerPortalResponse`).
- Migrated `app/billing/page.tsx`'s 12 billing call sites onto it — a net reduction in line count (128 lines changed, 55 added/73 removed) since each call site's boilerplate `fetch`+`res.ok`+`res.json()` collapsed to a single typed function call.

**A `null` vs `undefined` type gap the migration surfaced (same shape as the `analytics` pilot's `Record<string, number>` finding, different mechanism):** the backend's Pydantic schemas declare many optional fields as `Optional[X] = None`, which serialises to JSON `null` — but the page's pre-existing local `Subscription`/`Invoice`/`FeatureRow` interfaces (and a `fmtDate(iso?: string)` helper) declared those same fields as merely optional (`field?: string`, meaning `string | undefined`), never accounting for an explicit `null`. `tsc` correctly rejected assigning the accurately-typed API responses into that narrower local shape. Fixed by widening the affected fields (`trial_ends_at`, `current_period_end`, `notes`, `period_start`, `period_end`, `paid_at`, `stripe_hosted_url`, `stripe_pdf_url`, `created_at`, `category`, and `fmtDate`'s parameter) to `string | null` — matching what the backend actually sends, not a cosmetic type-check workaround.

**Verified:** every endpoint checked against a live local backend — registered a user, exercised the full customer-facing flow (plans/subscription/invoices/checkout/change-plan/portal/cancel), then updated that user's role directly in the dev SQLite DB to `admin`+`is_super_admin` to exercise the six admin-gated endpoints (list-all/update/activate/terminate/feature-matrix/toggle-feature) — all 13 endpoints' response bodies matched the new TypeScript interfaces exactly, including the `null` fields the type-widening fix above was based on. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, same 21 pre-existing warnings); `npm test` 19/19 (including `lib/pricing.test.ts`, confirming `pricing.ts` is genuinely untouched); `npm run build` succeeded, all 113 routes generated.
**Detail:** new `web/lib/api/billing.ts`; `web/app/billing/page.tsx` updated (storage/* calls and `lib/pricing.ts` deliberately left alone).

**Remaining:** 34 files/~175 call sites (governance's 3 sub-resources, onboarding, organisations, reports, screening, alerts, transactions, storage, etc.).

## C2 pass 14, 2026-09-16 (frontend API client — fourth pilot resource: `organisations`)

**Scope:** the fourth resource migrated onto the `lib/api/` pattern. `web/lib/signup.ts` was the single source of all `/api/v1/organisations*` calls (12 call sites across 11 exported functions, `setOrganisationIndustry`/`setOrganisationRiskProfile` being thin wrappers around `updateOrganisation` with no fetch of their own). Unlike the earlier pilots, `lib/signup.ts` also owns two genuinely different resources in the same file: `/api/v1/auth/*` (`registerAccount`, `confirmEmailVerification` — belongs to `auth.py`) and `/api/v1/aml-program/versions` (`getLatestAmlProgramDocument` — belongs to the separate `app/api/routes/aml_program.py`, prefix `/aml-program` with no `org_id` in the path, despite the similarly-named nested `/organisations/{org_id}/aml-program/*` routes this pilot does cover). Both were left untouched.

**A deliberate exclusion, not an oversight:** `exportAmlProgramHtml()` returns raw HTML (`HTMLResponse` on the backend, `r.text()` on the frontend) rather than JSON — the shared `apiGet`/`apiPost` primitives in `lib/api/client.ts` always call `.json()`, so this one function stays on raw `apiFetch()` rather than adding a one-off raw-passthrough primitive to the shared client for a single caller.

**What changed:**
- **`web/lib/api/organisations.ts`** (new) — the 11 in-scope typed functions (`listMyOrganisations`, `createOrganisation`, `updateOrganisation`, `setOrganisationIndustry`, `setOrganisationRiskProfile`, `selectIndustry`, `generateAmlProgram`, `generateRiskAssessment`, `listAmlProgramVersions`, `getAmlProgramVersion`, `exportAmlProgram`, `getAmlProgramHealth`, `acknowledgeAmlAccountability`) plus their type definitions (`Organisation`, `AmlProgram`, `AmlProgramItem`, `AmlProgramSectionCompletion`, `AmlProgramDocument`, `RiskFactor`, `RiskAssessment`, `AmlProgramVersion`, `AmlProgramVersionList`, `AmlProgramVersionDetail`, `ProgramHealth`), built on `apiGet`/`apiPost`/`apiPatch`. Types carried over from `lib/signup.ts`'s pre-existing local definitions unchanged — they were already accurately derived from the backend.
- **`web/lib/signup.ts`** — the 11 functions/11 types above are now imported from `lib/api/organisations.ts` and re-exported (not just deleted), since three existing consumers (`app/start-trial/StartTrialForm.tsx`, `app/aml-program/page.tsx`, `components/OnboardingWizard.tsx`) import them by name from `'@/lib/signup'` and weren't in scope to touch. `registerAccount`, `confirmEmailVerification`, `getLatestAmlProgramDocument`, `exportAmlProgramHtml`, the local `asJson()` helper, and `createFirstCustomer`/`FirstCustomerInput` (already migrated onto `lib/api/customers.ts` in pass 11) are unchanged. Net: 194 lines removed, 59 added.

**A discrepancy noted, not fixed:** the backend's real `OrganisationResponse` (`app/schemas/organisation.py`) has `industry_type` as a required field and also returns `status`, `created_at`, `aml_accountability_ack`/`_at`/`_by` — none of which the frontend's pre-existing `Organisation` type declares (or declares as optional when the backend has it required). Left as-is: `lib/signup.ts`/`lib/api/organisations.ts` are pure data-layer modules with no rendering logic of their own, so TypeScript's structural typing (extra/optional fields on the source side are harmless when assigned via a narrower-typed variable) keeps every consumer compiling; confirmed against a live backend response that the real payload is a superset of the declared type, not a mismatch.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, same 21 pre-existing warnings); `npm test` 19/19; `npm run build` succeeded, all 113 routes generated. Live-backend verification against a fresh local SQLite backend: registered test users and walked the full chain — list/patch org, `select-industry` (confirmed the enum-validated `industry_type` values, distinct from the free-text `industry_id` `updateOrganisation` sets), set `industry_id`+`risk_profile` via `updateOrganisation` (both required before `generate` endpoints will run), `generateAmlProgram`, `generateRiskAssessment`, `listAmlProgramVersions`, `getAmlProgramVersion`, `getAmlProgramHealth`, `exportAmlProgram`, `acknowledgeAmlAccountability` — every response body matched the corresponding TypeScript interface exactly, including `null` fields, the checklist `locked`/`is_preview` preview-paywall shape, and the version-list/version-detail split.
**Detail:** new `web/lib/api/organisations.ts`; `web/lib/signup.ts` updated (auth/aml-program-document/export-html calls deliberately left alone).

**Remaining:** 33 files/~163 call sites (governance's 3 sub-resources, onboarding, reports, screening, alerts, transactions, storage, etc.).

## C2 pass 15, 2026-09-16 (frontend API client — fifth pilot resource: `storage`)

**Scope:** the fifth resource migrated onto the `lib/api/` pattern, and the smallest so far — `app/billing/page.tsx`'s 6 `/api/v1/storage/*` call sites, deliberately left out of the `billing` pilot (C2 pass 13) since it's a genuinely separate backend resource (`app/api/routes/storage.py`, bring-your-own document storage config) surfaced as a tab on the billing page. After this pass, `app/billing/page.tsx` no longer imports `apiFetch`/uses a local `API` constant at all — both were dead once storage was the only remaining raw-fetch caller in the file.

**A new HTTP verb, not previously needed:** the storage config endpoints use `PUT` (idempotent replace), not `PATCH` — every prior pilot's mutating endpoints happened to be `POST`/`PATCH`. Added `apiPut<T>` to `lib/api/client.ts` alongside the existing `apiGet`/`apiPost`/`apiPatch`/`apiDelete`, mirroring `apiPatch`'s implementation exactly.

**A real bug the typing surfaced (same shape as the `customers` pilot's crash, different consequence):** both storage panels populated form state via `setForm({ backend: data.backend, ...data })`, spreading the *entire* API response — including `configured: boolean` and `verified: boolean | null` — into a `Record<string, string>` meant to hold only the backend-specific form fields. This compiled silently before migration because `data` came from a raw, untyped `res.json()`; once `data` was properly typed as `StorageConfig`, `tsc` correctly rejected it. Fixed by adding a `toFormFields()` helper that filters the response down to its actual string-valued fields, rather than a type-level workaround — a `Record<string,string>` should never have held a `boolean` field regardless of whether `tsc` had been catching it.

**What changed:**
- **`web/lib/api/client.ts`** — added `apiPut<T>`.
- **`web/lib/api/storage.ts`** (new) — all 6 endpoints (`getMyStorageConfig`, `setMyStorageConfig`, `clearMyStorageConfig`, `adminGetStorageConfig`, `adminSetStorageConfig`, `adminClearStorageConfig`). `StorageConfig` mirrors `app/schemas/storage.py`'s `StorageConfigResponse` with the optional fields widened to `| null` (the backend's `Optional[str] = None` fields serialize as explicit JSON `null`, confirmed live — same discipline as every prior pilot's null-vs-undefined fix, this time applied up front rather than caught by `tsc` after the fact). `StorageConfigInput` stays a plain `Record<string, string>`, matching how the page's own form state already models the backend-specific field sets (s3/azure/gcs each take different fields, including write-only secrets that never round-trip back in a response).
- **`web/app/billing/page.tsx`** — `MyStoragePanel`'s and `AdminStoragePanel`'s 6 call sites migrated; local `StorageConfig` interface removed in favour of the shared one; dead `apiFetch` import and local `API` constant removed.

**A backend gap noted, not fixed (pre-existing, unrelated to this migration):** `PUT`/`DELETE /storage/config` and the `/storage/admin/{industry_id}` equivalents 404 with `"No tenant found for this industry"` for a freshly `/auth/register`'d org in local dev — `storage_config_service._get_tenant()` looks up a separate `industry_tenants` row that registration doesn't seed, distinct from the `Organisation`/`Subscription` records it does create. Confirmed this is a dev/test-fixture gap, not a typing or migration issue, by seeding an `industry_tenants` row directly and re-running the full round trip successfully (see Verified).

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, same 21 pre-existing warnings); `npm test` 19/19; `npm run build` succeeded, all 113 routes generated. Live-backend verification against a fresh local SQLite backend: confirmed the unconfigured-default GET shape on both `/config` and `/admin/{id}`, the `"No tenant found"`/`"Tenant not found"` error responses parse correctly into `ApiError` via the shared client, then seeded an `industry_tenants` row directly and re-ran the full chain — `PUT` (s3, then azure backend) → `GET` (reflects the write) → `DELETE` (reverts to default) on both the self-service and admin (super-admin-promoted user) endpoints — every response matched `StorageConfig` exactly, including the `verified: true`/`false` connectivity-check result and encrypted-but-not-round-tripped secret fields (`secret_key`/`account_key` correctly absent from responses).
**Detail:** `web/lib/api/client.ts` (+`apiPut`); new `web/lib/api/storage.ts`; `web/app/billing/page.tsx` updated (now fully migrated — no raw `apiFetch`/`API` calls left in the file).

**Remaining:** 32 files/~157 call sites (governance's 3 sub-resources, onboarding, reports, screening, alerts, transactions, etc.).

## C2 pass 16, 2026-09-16 (frontend API client — sixth pilot resource: `governance/training`)

**Scope:** the sixth resource migrated onto the `lib/api/` pattern, and the first of governance's three sub-resources (training/policies/controls — each a separate pilot, per the original survey). `web/app/governance/training/page.tsx`'s 11 `/api/v1/governance/training/*` call sites, all confined to one file — the backend route package (`app/api/routes/governance/training/{courses,assignments,records,dashboard}.py`, composed under one router in `__init__.py`) is itself already split by concern, which made mapping response shapes to types straightforward.

**A deliberate exclusion, not an oversight:** the certificate export (`GET .../records/{id}/certificate-html`) is a plain `<a href>` browser navigation in the page, not a fetch call site, and returns print-ready HTML (`HTMLResponse` on the backend) rather than JSON. Rather than a fetch function, `lib/api/governanceTraining.ts` exports `certificateHtmlUrl(recordId)` — a plain string-builder using the shared client's `API_BASE` — so the page's `<a href>` stays a direct navigation instead of being forced through a fetch+blob dance it doesn't need.

**What changed:**
- **`web/lib/api/governanceTraining.ts`** (new) — 10 typed functions (`listCourses`, `listRecords`, `getTrainingDashboard`, `getComplianceReport`, `seedStandardCourses`, `seedIndustryPack`, `completeTraining`, `retakeTraining`, `renewTraining`, `createAssignment`) plus `certificateHtmlUrl()`, built on `apiGet`/`apiPost`. Types (`Course`, `TrainingRecord`, `TrainingDashboard`, `ComplianceReport`, `Assignment`, etc.) mirror the route package's `_course_dict`/`_record_dict`/`_assignment_dict` helpers and each endpoint's actual return shape — scoped to the fields the page renders, not the full course/record dict (which carries several more fields, e.g. `learning_objectives`, `provider`, `external_url`, that no view in this page uses).
- **`web/app/governance/training/page.tsx`** — all 11 call sites migrated; local `Course`/`TrainingRecord`/`TrainingStatus` type declarations removed in favour of the shared ones. The original `fetchAll()`'s per-call fallback semantics (each of courses/records/dashboard independently keeps its demo-data default if that one fetch fails, rather than an all-or-nothing `Promise.all`) were preserved by switching to `Promise.allSettled` and checking each settled result individually, rather than collapsing to a single try/catch that would have changed the failure behavior.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean (one iteration: forgot to import the now-shared `TrainingStatus` type used by the page's `STATUS_COLOR` map, caught immediately and fixed); `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, same 21 pre-existing warnings); `npm test` 19/19; `npm run build` succeeded, all 113 routes generated. Live-backend verification against a fresh local SQLite backend with a role-promoted (`admin`) test user: seeded the standard course catalogue and an industry pack, created a bulk assignment (which also creates the individual training record), then walked a record through complete (fail) → retake → complete (pass) → renew, fetched its certificate HTML, and checked the dashboard and compliance-report endpoints — every response matched the new TypeScript interfaces exactly. One field added after live testing: `ComplianceReport` was missing the `disclaimer` field the real endpoint always returns (harmless via structural typing, but added for accuracy to match `TrainingDashboard`'s equivalent field).
**Detail:** new `web/lib/api/governanceTraining.ts`; `web/app/governance/training/page.tsx` updated (now fully migrated — no raw `apiFetch`/`API` calls left in the file).

**Remaining:** 31 files/~146 call sites (governance's remaining 2 sub-resources — policies, controls —, onboarding, reports, screening, alerts, transactions, etc.).

## C2 pass 17, 2026-09-16 (frontend API client — seventh pilot resource: `governance/policies`)

**Scope:** the seventh resource migrated onto the `lib/api/` pattern, and the second of governance's three sub-resources. `web/app/governance/policies/page.tsx`'s 5 `/api/v1/governance/policies*` call sites, all in one file.

**A deliberate exclusion, not an oversight:** the PDF/HTML export (`GET .../policies/{id}/export-html`) is a `window.open()` browser navigation in the page, not a fetch call site, and returns print-ready HTML rather than JSON — same shape as the `governance/training` pilot's certificate export. `lib/api/governancePolicies.ts` exports `policyExportHtmlUrl(policyId)`, a plain string-builder, rather than a fetch function.

**What changed:**
- **`web/lib/api/governancePolicies.ts`** (new) — 4 typed functions (`listPolicies`, `listPolicyVersions`, `runPolicyWorkflowAction`, `createPolicy`) plus `policyExportHtmlUrl()`, built on `apiGet`/`apiPost`. Types (`Policy`, `PolicyVersion`, `PolicyCreateInput`) mirror `app/schemas/governance.py`'s `PolicyResponse`/`PolicyVersionResponse`/`PolicyCreate`.
- **`web/app/governance/policies/page.tsx`** — all 5 call sites migrated; local `Policy`/`PolicyType`/`PolicyStatus` type declarations and the loosely-typed `versions: any[]` state removed in favour of the shared types.

**A real bug the typing surfaced (a display gap, not a crash — same discovery method as the `organisations` pilot's `industry_type` discrepancy):** the page's pre-existing local `PolicyType` union (and its `TYPE_LABELS` map) was missing `independent_review_policy` — a real, currently-in-use enum value on the backend (`app/models/governance_policies.py`). Every fresh org auto-seeds an "Independent Review Policy" document at onboarding, confirmed live: its `policy_type` came back as `"independent_review_policy"` in the very first `GET /governance/policies` call in verification. Before this fix, `TYPE_LABELS[p.policy_type]` would have silently rendered `undefined` in that policy's row for every real org — a genuine display bug the narrower hand-typed union let through unnoticed. Fixed by adding the missing member to both the type and the label map.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, same 21 pre-existing warnings); `npm test` 19/19; `npm run build` succeeded, all 113 routes generated. Live-backend verification against a fresh local SQLite backend with a role-promoted (`admin`) test user: confirmed the auto-seeded policy set on `GET /governance/policies` (which is where the `independent_review_policy` gap surfaced), walked one policy through the full workflow chain (`submit_for_review` → `submit_for_compliance` → `submit_for_approval` → `publish`, confirming the version-bump and version-snapshot side effects), checked `GET .../versions` before and after publish, created a new policy via `POST`, and fetched the HTML export — every response matched the TypeScript types exactly.
**Detail:** new `web/lib/api/governancePolicies.ts`; `web/app/governance/policies/page.tsx` updated (now fully migrated — no raw `apiFetch`/`API` calls left in the file).

**Remaining:** 30 files/~141 call sites (governance's last sub-resource — controls —, onboarding, reports, screening, alerts, transactions, etc.).

## C2 pass 18, 2026-09-17 (frontend API client — eighth pilot resource: `governance/controls`, closing out governance)

**Scope:** the eighth resource migrated onto the `lib/api/` pattern, and the third and last of governance's three sub-resources — training (pass 16), policies (pass 17), and now controls complete the set. `web/app/governance/controls/page.tsx`'s 4 `/api/v1/governance/controls*` call sites, all in one file. The backend route file's much larger surface (findings, remediations, evidence, open-remediations) is untouched by this pilot since the page never calls those endpoints — not a deliberate exclusion so much as scope matching what actually exists to migrate.

**What changed:**
- **`web/lib/api/governanceControls.ts`** (new) — 4 typed functions (`listControls`, `listControlTests`, `recordControlTest`, `createControl`), built on `apiGet`/`apiPost`. Types (`Control`, `ControlTest`, `ControlCreateInput`, `ControlTestInput`) mirror `app/schemas/governance.py`'s `ControlResponse`/`ControlTestResponse`/`ControlCreate`/`ControlTestCreate`, scoped to the fields this page renders. Unlike the `policies` pilot, the page's pre-existing local `RiskArea`/`ControlStatus`/`Effectiveness`/`Frequency` unions were already complete and accurate against the backend enums (`app/models/governance_controls.py`) — checked all five against the real enum members before reusing them verbatim, no gaps found this time.
- **`web/app/governance/controls/page.tsx`** — all 4 call sites migrated; local `Control` interface and the loosely-typed `tests: any[]` state removed in favour of the shared types.

**A real bug the typing surfaced (same class as the `storage` pilot's `toFormFields` fix): `recordTest`'s demo-data fallback path** (used when the real POST fails) synthesized a test object with only 6 of `ControlTest`'s 13 fields — missing `control_id`, `tester_id`, `action_required`, `retest_required`, `is_finalised`. This compiled silently before migration since `tests` was `any[]`; once properly typed, `tsc` correctly rejected the incomplete object. Fixed by completing the demo object with the same field values the real backend would plausibly return, rather than loosening the type.

**A behavior quirk noted, not fixed (pre-existing, unrelated to this migration):** confirmed live that `POST .../tests` creates the test record with `is_finalised: false` and `calculated_effectiveness: null` — the control's own `effectiveness` field does *not* update until a separate finalisation step (not called by this page). The page's optimistic UI update (`recordTest` immediately sets local `effectiveness` state to `"effective"`/`"ineffective"`) was already making this assumption before the migration; unchanged here since it's an existing application-behavior question, not a typing defect.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean (one iteration: the demo-fallback bug above, caught and fixed); `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, same 21 pre-existing warnings); `npm test` 19/19; `npm run build` succeeded, all 113 routes generated. Live-backend verification against a fresh local SQLite backend with a role-promoted (`admin`) test user: confirmed the auto-seeded control set on `GET /governance/controls`, recorded a passing test via `POST .../tests` and confirmed it in `GET .../tests`, and created a new control via `POST /governance/controls` — every response matched the TypeScript types exactly, including every `risk_area`/`frequency`/`control_type`/`status`/`effectiveness` enum value seen.
**Detail:** new `web/lib/api/governanceControls.ts`; `web/app/governance/controls/page.tsx` updated (now fully migrated — no raw `apiFetch`/`API` calls left in the file). **This closes out all three governance sub-resources (training/policies/controls).**

**Remaining:** 29 files/~137 call sites (onboarding, reports, screening, alerts, transactions, etc.).

## C2 pass 20, 2026-09-17 (frontend API client — tenth pilot resource: `alerts`)

**Scope:** the tenth resource migrated onto the `lib/api/` pattern. `web/app/monitoring/page.tsx`'s 5 `/api/v1/alerts*` call sites — the page also calls `/api/v1/transactions/*` (2 sites, a genuinely separate resource sharing the same file, left for its own pilot) and `app/api/routes/alerts.py`'s own surface has more endpoints (`assign`, `flag-smr`, `record-result`, `recommendations`) the page never calls, so neither is a deliberate exclusion so much as scope matching what the page actually needs. Also confirmed `app/api/routes/alerts.py` (prefix `/alerts`, `TransactionAlert`) is a genuinely different resource from `app/api/routes/screening/alerts.py` (mounted under `/screening/alerts`, `ScreeningAlert`) despite the similar name — same naming-collision discipline as prior pilots.

**A real bug the typing surfaced:** the backend's `GET /alerts` (list) and the mutation endpoints (`review`/`escalate`) return two different response shapes — `AlertListOut` (a slim summary) vs. `AlertOut` (the full record, with `transaction_id`, `rule_name`, `description`, `alert_type`, `created_at`, `resolved_at` that the list shape doesn't have). The page's `mapAlert()` helper already handled both shapes correctly at runtime (via `??` fallbacks) but was typed `raw: any`, which hid a second, real bug: it read `raw.alert_id` as a fallback for `raw.alert_ref`, but neither backend response shape ever has an `alert_id` field — dead code that always evaluated to `undefined` and was masked by the loose typing. Removed the dead fallback. Also widened the page's local `Alert` interface's `transaction_id`/`rule_name`/`assigned_to`/`resolved_at` fields to `| null` to match the backend's real `Optional[X] = None` nullability (same discipline as every prior pilot's null-vs-undefined fix).

**What changed:**
- **`web/lib/api/alerts.ts`** (new) — 5 typed functions (`listAlerts`, `getAlertDashboard`, `reviewAlert`, `escalateAlert`, `createCaseFromAlert`), built on `apiGet`/`apiPost`. Types (`AlertListItem`, `AlertDetail`, `AlertDashboard`, `CreateCaseFromAlertResponse`) mirror `app/schemas/monitoring.py`'s `AlertListOut`/`AlertOut` and the route file's dashboard/create-case response dicts.
- **`web/app/monitoring/page.tsx`** — its 5 alerts call sites migrated; `mapAlert()` retyped to accept a `RawAlert` type — `(AlertListItem | AlertDetail) & Partial<Pick<AlertDetail, ...>>` — that honestly represents which fields are genuinely absent on the list shape, rather than reverting to `any`; local `Alert` interface's nullable fields widened per the bug above. The `transactions/*` call sites and their local `API`/`apiFetch` usage are untouched.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean (one iteration: the dead `alert_id` fallback and the nullable-field gaps, both caught and fixed); `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, same 21 pre-existing warnings); `npm test` 19/19; `npm run build` succeeded, all 113 routes generated. Live-backend verification against a fresh local SQLite backend with a role-promoted (`admin`) test user: created a customer and a large transaction (which auto-generates an alert via the monitoring engine), confirmed the list and dashboard shapes, then reviewed one alert (`cleared`) and — via a second customer/transaction, after hitting an unrelated pre-existing backend bug on a second transaction for the same customer (a naive-vs-aware datetime comparison in `update_behaviour_profile`, noted but not fixed as out of scope) — escalated a second alert and created a case from it. Every response matched the TypeScript types exactly.
**Detail:** new `web/lib/api/alerts.ts`; `web/app/monitoring/page.tsx` updated (alerts call sites fully migrated; transactions call sites deliberately untouched).

**Remaining:** 26 files/~129 call sites (onboarding, reports, transactions, etc.).

## C2 pass 22, 2026-09-17 (frontend API client — twelfth pilot resource: `reports`)

**Scope:** the twelfth resource, covering both `app/reporting/page.tsx` (ttr/smr/summary + their shared review/approve/submit/acknowledge workflow) and `app/ecdd/page.tsx` (list/create/decide) — both mount under the same backend router package (`app/api/routes/reports/`, prefix `/reports`), so treated as one pilot despite being two frontend pages, same discipline as the `billing` pilot covering one full backend resource across its sub-areas.

**A deliberate exclusion, not an oversight:** `app/reporting/page.tsx` also calls `/api/v1/ifti/*` (a genuinely separate resource — `app/api/routes/ifti.py`, its own router/prefix, not part of the `reports` package) through the exact same generic review/approve/submit/acknowledge workflow function. Rather than typing `ifti` too (out of scope for this pilot) or leaving the whole shared function untyped, `advanceStatus()` was split into an `if (report.report_type === "ifti")` branch (untouched, raw `apiFetch`/`API`) and an `else` branch (`ttr`/`smr`, now typed) — TypeScript narrows `report.report_type` to `'ttr' | 'smr'` in the `else` branch automatically once `ifti` is excluded by the `if`.

**Two real bugs the typing surfaced:**
1. The workflow-action functions (`review`/`approve`/`submit`/`acknowledge`) were initially typed to return the full `TtrReport | SmrReport` shape — but live verification showed all four actually return a small ad-hoc `{report_id, status}` dict (`submit` adds `disclaimer`; `mlro-sign-off` adds `mlro_sign_off`), confirmed directly against `app/api/routes/reports/ttr.py`'s and `smr.py`'s literal `return {...}` statements. Fixed by introducing a `ReportWorkflowResult` type matching backend reality. Harmless in practice since the page discards the response body on success (it applies its own optimistic status update) — but the type would have been actively wrong for any future caller.
2. `ECDDDecisionInput.status` was initially over-narrowed to a `'pending' | 'completed' | 'rejected'` literal union (matching the backend schema's inline comment) — but `app/schemas/report.py`'s `ECDDDecisionRequest.status` is actually a plain `str` field, not a Pydantic enum/Literal. The over-narrow type broke the existing `DecisionPanel` → `onDecide` prop chain (whose local `status` state is a plain `string`), which would have forced a wider, unrelated retyping cascade to "fix." Reverted to `string`, matching the real (looser) backend contract instead of the comment's aspiration.

**What changed:**
- **`web/lib/api/reports.ts`** (new) — `listTtrReports`/`listSmrReports`/`getReportingSummary`/`reviewReport`/`approveReport`/`submitReport`/`acknowledgeReport` (generic across `ttr`/`smr` via a `reportType` parameter, mirroring the `governance/policies` pilot's `runPolicyWorkflowAction` pattern) plus `listEcddRecords`/`createEcddRecord`/`decideEcddRecord`. Types (`TtrReport`, `SmrReport`, `ReportingSummary`, `ReportWorkflowResult`, `ECDDRecord`, `ECDDCreateInput`, `ECDDDecisionInput`) mirror the backend's `_ttr_dict`/`_smr_dict` helpers (scoped to the ~18 fields `app/reporting/page.tsx`'s `mapReport()` actually reads — the real SMR dict has ~60 AUSTRAC-form fields), `reporting_summary()`'s shape, and `app/schemas/report.py`'s `ECDDCreate`/`ECDDResponse`/`ECDDDecisionRequest`.
- **`web/app/reporting/page.tsx`** — `fetchData()` switched from `Promise.all` to `Promise.allSettled` to preserve the original per-source tolerance (any one of ifti/ttr/smr/summary can fail independently without blanking the others); `advanceStatus()` split as described above.
- **`web/app/ecdd/page.tsx`** — all 3 call sites migrated; local `ECDDRecord` interface and `apiFetch`/`API` removed entirely (now fully migrated).

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean (two iterations: the workflow-result and ECDD-status-narrowing bugs above, both caught and fixed); `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, same 21 pre-existing warnings); `npm test` 19/19; `npm run build` succeeded, all 113 routes generated. Live-backend verification against a fresh local SQLite backend: generated a TTR from a real transaction, confirmed `listTtrReports`/`getReportingSummary` shapes, then walked `review` → `approve` (using a second admin user in the same org to satisfy the backend's maker-checker rule — same reviewer can't also approve) → `submit` (hit a real, expected AUSTRAC-field validation error, confirming `ApiError` propagation) → `acknowledge`; confirmed the empty-list shape for `listSmrReports`; created, listed, and decided an ECDD record end-to-end. Every response matched the TypeScript types exactly, including the workflow-action fix above.
**Detail:** new `web/lib/api/reports.ts`; `web/app/reporting/page.tsx` updated (ttr/smr/summary migrated, ifti deliberately untouched); `web/app/ecdd/page.tsx` updated (fully migrated).

**Remaining:** 23 files/~117 call sites (onboarding, ifti, etc.).

## C2 pass 23, 2026-09-17 (frontend API client — thirteenth pilot resource: `onboarding`)

**Scope:** the thirteenth resource, covering `app/onboarding/page.tsx`'s (the authenticated operator dashboard) and `components/Onboarding/BulkUpload.tsx`'s `/api/v1/onboarding/*` call sites — both go through `apiFetch()` (Bearer token/CSRF).

**A deliberate exclusion, not an oversight:** `app/onboarding/[token]/page.tsx` and `components/Onboarding/CustomerPortal.tsx` hit the same route file's `/api/v1/onboarding/portal/{token}/*` endpoints, but through plain, unauthenticated `fetch()` — a public, token-based flow for the applicant's own browser session, not the operator's. Genuinely different auth model, left untouched, same discipline as every other auth-boundary exclusion in this migration. `components/Onboarding/DocumentUploadStep.tsx`'s own `POST /api/v1/documents` call was also left alone — a separate resource (general document storage, not `/onboarding/*`) that happens to live in an onboarding-flow component.

**A structural pattern not seen in the prior twelve pilots:** `app/onboarding/page.tsx`'s child components (`ApplicantTable.tsx`, `DocumentUploadStep.tsx`, `ScreeningStep.tsx`) each independently declare their own local `Session` interface for props, rather than sharing one type — so importing the new client's `OnboardingSession` directly as the page's own `Session` type broke prop-passing to all three ("two different types with this name exist, but they are unrelated"; TypeScript scopes locally-named interfaces per-file, it doesn't unify them across a props boundary). Resolved by keeping the page's own local `Session` interface separate from the client's `OnboardingSession` (not an alias), widening only its nullable fields (`sanctions_match`/`risk_level`/`risk_score`/`created_at`/`customer_id`) to match backend reality, and separately widening the same fields in each child component's own local `Session` interface — relying on structural typing (the wider API response satisfies each narrower local shape) rather than unifying every consumer onto one shared type, a larger change than this pilot's scope.

**What changed:**
- **`web/lib/api/onboarding.ts`** (new) — `listOnboardingSessions`, `getOnboardingStats`, `sendOnboardingReminder`, `cancelOnboardingSession`, `createOnboardingSession`, `importOnboardingFile` (CSV/Excel, picked by file extension, reusing `apiPost`'s existing `FormData` passthrough rather than adding a new primitive). Types (`OnboardingSession`, `OnboardingSessionDetail`, `CreateSessionInput`, `PipelineStats`, `BatchSummary`, `BatchImportError`) mirror `app/schemas/onboarding.py`'s `SessionSummary`/`SessionDetail`/`SessionCreate`/`BatchSummary`/`PipelineStats`.
- **`web/app/onboarding/page.tsx`** — `fetchData()` switched from a bespoke fetch pair to `Promise.allSettled` over `listOnboardingSessions`/`getOnboardingStats`; `handleRemind`/`handleCancel`/`handleManualSubmit` migrated to the new client; local `Session` interface's nullable fields widened as described above; `apiFetch`/`API` no longer referenced anywhere in the file.
- **`web/components/Onboarding/BulkUpload.tsx`** — `handleFile` migrated to `importOnboardingFile`; local `UploadResult` interface replaced by the client's `BatchSummary`.
- **`web/components/Onboarding/ApplicantTable.tsx`**, **`ScreeningStep.tsx`** — no API calls of their own; only their local `Session` interfaces' nullability widened to accept the now-accurately-typed data flowing down from the parent.
- **`web/components/Onboarding/DocumentUploadStep.tsx`** — same nullability widening on its local `Session` interface (`customer_id`); its own `/api/v1/documents` call and `apiFetch` import deliberately untouched.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean (two iterations: the `OnboardingSession`-as-`Session` alias breaking three child components' own local `Session` types, and one child — `ScreeningStep.tsx` — not caught by the first grep pass, both found and fixed); `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, same 21 pre-existing warnings); `npm test` 19/19; `npm run build` succeeded, all 113 routes generated. Live-backend verification against a fresh local SQLite backend with a role-promoted (`admin`) test user: created a session via `createOnboardingSession`, confirmed `listOnboardingSessions`/`getOnboardingStats` shapes, sent a reminder and cancelled the session (confirming `OnboardingSessionDetail`'s shape), then imported a CSV both with a valid row (`success_rows`) and an invalid one (`error_rows`, confirming `BatchImportError`'s exact `{row, data, error}` shape against a real per-row validation failure). Every response matched the TypeScript types exactly. (Noted, not a bug: the session's returned `industry_id` came back as the org id rather than the `digital-currency-exchange` sent in the create payload — by design, per the route file's own comment that `industry_id`/`organisation_id` are always taken from the authenticated user's tenant scope, not the request body.)
**Detail:** new `web/lib/api/onboarding.ts`; `web/app/onboarding/page.tsx`, `web/components/Onboarding/BulkUpload.tsx` updated (fully migrated for their respective call sites); `web/components/Onboarding/ApplicantTable.tsx`, `ScreeningStep.tsx`, `DocumentUploadStep.tsx` updated (type-only nullability fixes, no call-site changes).

**Remaining:** 21 files/~111 call sites (ifti, documents, etc.).

## C2 pass 24, 2026-09-17 (frontend API client — fourteenth pilot resource: `documents`)

**Scope:** the fourteenth resource, closing out the exclusion left from the `onboarding` pilot (C2 pass 23). Covers `app/documents/page.tsx` (the document vault — list/stats/upload/download/archive/delete, 6 call sites) and `components/Onboarding/DocumentUploadStep.tsx` (the operator-assisted onboarding upload, 1 call site) — both hit the same backend resource, `app/api/routes/documents.py`.

**A new primitive, not a new pattern:** the download endpoint (`GET /documents/{id}/download`) returns raw file bytes with a `Content-Disposition` header, not JSON — the shared client's `apiGet`/etc. all parse JSON and throw on non-2xx via `ApiError`, which doesn't fit a binary response. Added `downloadDocument()` to the new `lib/api/documents.ts` as a thin wrapper directly over `apiFetch()` that hands back the raw `Response`, letting the caller check `.ok` and call `.blob()` itself exactly as the page did pre-migration — same "call apiFetch directly for reasons `client.ts` doesn't help with" discipline as `onboarding.ts`'s `importOnboardingFile()` reusing `apiPost`'s `FormData` passthrough, just the download-side equivalent.

**What changed:**
- **`web/lib/api/documents.ts`** (new) — `listDocuments`, `getDocumentStats`, `uploadDocument`, `downloadDocument`, `archiveDocument`, `deleteDocument`. Types (`Doc`, `DocumentStats`) mirror `app/schemas/document.py`'s `DocumentResponse` and `app/services/document_service.py`'s `document_stats()` dict — `by_category` is enum-keyed at runtime (confirmed live: 15 category keys, more than the frontend's own 9-entry `CATEGORIES` display list), so `Record<string, number>`, same call as the `analytics` pilot's `by_status`/`by_risk` dicts.
- **`web/app/documents/page.tsx`** — all 6 call sites migrated; `DEMO_DOCS`' fallback array given the now-required `id: number` field; `fileIcon()`/`relTime()`'s parameter types widened to accept `| null` (the backend's `Optional[X] = None` fields, same recurring null-vs-undefined gap as every other pilot); local `Doc` interface and `apiFetch`/`API` removed entirely; the original demo-data-fallback semantics preserved exactly (a failed document list drops the whole page to demo mode, a failed stats call alone does not).
- **`web/components/Onboarding/DocumentUploadStep.tsx`** — its 1 call site migrated to `uploadDocument()`; `apiFetch`/local `API` constant removed.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean (one iteration: `fileIcon`/`relTime`'s parameter types needed widening for the new nullable `mime_type`/`created_at` fields); `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, same 21 pre-existing warnings); `npm test` 19/19; `npm run build` succeeded, all 113 routes generated. Live-backend verification against a fresh local SQLite backend with a role-promoted (`admin`) test user: uploaded a real file (confirmed `Doc`'s exact shape), listed it and confirmed `DocumentStats`, downloaded it and diffed the response headers/body against what the page expects for its blob-download flow, archived it, then deleted it (204, confirmed gone from a re-list). Every response matched the TypeScript types exactly.
**Detail:** new `web/lib/api/documents.ts`; `web/app/documents/page.tsx`, `web/components/Onboarding/DocumentUploadStep.tsx` updated (both fully migrated for their respective call sites).

**Remaining:** 19 files/~104 call sites (ifti, independent-review, api-keys/webhooks, risk-matrix, etc.).

## C2 pass 25, 2026-09-18 (frontend API client — fifteenth pilot resource: `ifti`)

**Scope:** the fifteenth resource, closing out the last exclusion in `app/reporting/page.tsx` — its `/api/v1/ifti/*` calls, deliberately left on raw `apiFetch()`/`API` since the `reports` pilot (C2 pass 22) because IFTI is a genuinely separate backend resource (`app/api/routes/ifti.py`, its own router/prefix, not part of `app/api/routes/reports/`) despite sharing the page and the same generic review/approve/submit/acknowledge workflow shape as `ttr`/`smr`. After this pass, `app/reporting/page.tsx` has no raw `apiFetch`/`API` calls left in it at all.

**A contrast worth noting against the `reports` pilot's bug:** pass 22 found that `ttr`/`smr`'s workflow-action endpoints only return a small ad-hoc `{report_id, status}` dict, not the full report — `ifti`'s equivalent endpoints (`review`/`approve`/`submit`/`acknowledge`) really do return the full `IFTIResponse` record (`response_model=IFTIResponse`, `return r`), confirmed directly against `app/api/routes/ifti.py`'s literal return statements — so `IFTIRecord` (this pilot's response type) applies to all 5 functions, no separate workflow-result type needed here.

**What changed:**
- **`web/lib/api/ifti.ts`** (new) — `listIftiRecords`, `reviewIftiRecord`, `approveIftiRecord`, `submitIftiRecord`, `acknowledgeIftiRecord`. `IFTIRecord` mirrors `app/schemas/ifti.py`'s `IFTIResponse`, scoped to the ~15 fields `app/reporting/page.tsx`'s `mapReport()` actually reads (not the real ~25-field AUSTRAC IFTI-DRA record).
- **`web/app/reporting/page.tsx`** — `fetchData()`'s `ifti` branch switched from a raw `apiFetch()` call inside the existing `Promise.allSettled` to `listIftiRecords()`; `advanceStatus()`'s `if (report.report_type === "ifti")` branch (added in pass 22 to isolate this exclusion) now calls the typed functions instead of hand-building URLs; the now-unused `apiFetch` import and local `API` constant removed.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean on the first pass; `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, same 21 pre-existing warnings); `npm test` 19/19; `npm run build` succeeded, all 113 routes generated. Live-backend verification against a fresh local SQLite backend, using a second admin user in the same org to satisfy the same maker-checker rule (`_assert_maker_checker`) hit in the `reports` pilot: created a record, walked `review` → `approve`, then hit the same class of real AUSTRAC-field validation error (`reporter_full_name`/`reporter_austrac_id` required) confirmed legitimate in pass 22 — created a second record with those fields pre-populated and walked it all the way through `submit` → `acknowledge`. Every response matched `IFTIRecord` exactly.
**Detail:** new `web/lib/api/ifti.ts`; `web/app/reporting/page.tsx` updated (now fully migrated — no raw `apiFetch`/`API` calls left in the file at all, across the `reports` and `ifti` pilots).

**Remaining:** 18 files/~99 call sites (independent-review, api-keys/webhooks, risk-matrix, etc.).
